# 16c — Simulator backlog

Draft 0.1, 2026-09-20, for `main` at `820ad9af` (tree clean, deployed to dev1). Companion to
[doc 16](16-combat-arena.md) (the specification; §11 holds the as-built amendments) and
[16b](16b-combat-implementation-plan.md) (the plan that was built; §9 is the cut ledger). Every cut 16b
planned has landed: R to N, X.1 to X.6, F, Q, and the networking fix `820ad9af`.

**What this is.** The list the owner asked for on 2026-09-20: the outstanding bugs, the tech debt, and
the new functionality for the simulator, followed by the order in which they are built. It is executed
from the top of §4, with a commit and a push at each sub-cut and a ledger row in 16b §9 per cut.

**How it was built.** Four triage passes re-checked every open finding of the 2026-09-18 readiness
review (161 findings), every residual the cut F, X.6 and Q judges left, and the platform and operations
seeds against `820ad9af` by reading the code. Four hunts read the cut Q surfaces afresh (cockpit and
epistemics, the panel clients, the reflex AI and the lobby, the arena debts). One live smoke played eight
matches on the real `mushd` binary over SSH line mode, the websocket and the TUI. Two lenses proposed
features. Every bug and debt below was then handed to a verifier that tried to refute it; the priorities
here are the verifiers' corrected ones. Where several lenses found one defect it appears once. Where the
verifiers disagreed on a priority the higher confirmed one is used, because dropping a P1 costs more than
scheduling a P2 early.

**How to read it.** §1 and §2 are tables; an id (`B-nn`, `T-nn`) is stable and is what §4 cites. "Verified"
means a verifier confirmed it by reading the code at `820ad9af`; "plausible" means the symptom or the
code fact is confirmed and the scenario or the cause is not. "Live" means the smoke saw it on the running
server. Sizes: S is under about 150 lines with tests, M under about 600, L under about 2,000, XL more.
File paths are relative to `crates/` unless they start with `docs/`, `web/`, `deploy/`, `scripts/` or
`.github/`; `tacsim/src/` is abbreviated to `tacsim:` and `tacsim/src/arena/` to `arena:`.

**The rubric.** P0: a match cannot complete, a player is locked out, a record is corrupted, or hidden
information leaks to a player. P1: a wrong outcome or a confusing dead end in ordinary play (two humans,
or one human against the AI fill, on the shipped map, over line mode, the TUI or the web client). P2: an
edge case, a rare path or a staff-only surface. Debt: no player-visible defect.

**Counts.** 73 bugs (0 P0, 14 P1, 59 P2); 80 debts; 18 ranked features and 6 held for a design
conversation; 12 lettered cuts and an unlettered tail of 5. Since cut D's record (2026-09-21): 75 bugs
(`B-74` and `B-75` added, both P2) and 86 debts (`T-81` to `T-86` added), of which cut D closed 25 bugs
and 9 debts outright and one bug (`B-12`) and two debts (`T-59`, `T-60`) in part; a closed row keeps its
text and says where it landed in its id cell. After the owner-supplied external review of 2026-09-22: 80 bugs (`B-76` to `B-80`
added, all P2, each confirmed and narrowed by two lenses reading `c3852a0c`; the reviewer's probes were not
re-run), 86 debts unchanged, and a thirteenth cut, AA, placed after E and before B. Cut L's source review
at `8366b3fe` adds `B-81` (P2), bringing the total to 81 bugs (0 P0, 14 P1, 67 P2); no runtime probe
was run for that finding. The 2026-09-26 dev1 deploy recon adds `B-82` (P1, the split's world task never
holds the LLM service; read at `50397c1b` and seen live in dev1's journal), bringing the total to 82 bugs (0 P0,
15 P1, 67 P2), and the owner's fix cut AC is placed after I; cut AC's record adds `B-83` (P2, a `BOARD` lost on the split at the walk's commit window) and `T-89` (no per-feature LLM budget caps on the features dev1 now spends on), 83 bugs and 89 debts; cut AE's sweep adds `B-84` (P1, the reflex controller's two path-search wastes), 84 bugs (0 P0, 16 P1, 68 P2), and its fix cut AI is placed after AF. The 2026-09-29 deploy verification added `B-85` (P2, the split's `/healthz` blind to the world loop), 85 bugs (0 P0, 16 P1, 69 P2). The 2026-10-02 engine-lessons sweep over the six bot cuts (AJ to AO) adds `B-86` to `B-103` (eighteen bugs: `B-86` and `B-87` P1, the rest P2), `T-90` to `T-99` (ten debts), four ranked features (`N19` to `N22`) and the owner questions `Q45` to `Q57`, bringing the totals to 103 bugs (0 P0, 18 P1, 85 P2), 99 debts and 22 ranked features; the owner's cut AP (the engine takes the bot's lessons) is placed after AO. Cut E's record
(2026-09-22) closed ten bugs (`B-04`, `B-05`, `B-39` to `B-46`) and six debts (`T-32`, `T-46`, `T-47`,
`T-55`, `T-56`, `T-81`) outright and one debt (`T-42`) in part; it added no row, its residuals being listed
in 16b section 9, row E, and its deferrals in section 4's cut E entry. Cut AA's record (2026-09-22) closed
its three bugs (`B-76`, `B-78`, `B-79`) and added one owner question (`Q41`, the default AA.3 was built
on); its residuals are in 16b section 9, row AA, and its deferrals in section 4's cut AA entry.
Cut L closes five bugs and seventeen debts outright, and corrects T-54's
comment while leaving its per-channel leases open. Its rows retain the historical
problem, location and proposed payment columns; validation and integration are
recorded in the Cut L entry and PR #1.
Cut B's record (2026-09-23) closed nineteen bugs (`B-08`, `B-09`, `B-10`, `B-13`, `B-14`, `B-23`, `B-24`,
`B-26`, `B-38`, `B-59` to `B-67`, `B-80`) and ten debts (`T-33`, `T-43`, `T-44`, `T-58`, `T-61` to `T-65`, `T-86`)
outright, `T-60`'s two carried slips, and two debts (`T-31`, `T-42`) in part; it added no row, its residuals
being listed in 16b section 9, row B, and its deferrals in section 4's cut B entry.
Cut M's record (2026-09-23) closed fourteen bugs (`B-27`, `B-47` to `B-57`, `B-74`, `B-77`) and twenty-four
debts (`T-01` to `T-07`, `T-09` to `T-17`, `T-23`, `T-26` to `T-28`, `T-80`, `T-82` to `T-84`) outright, `T-42`
in part (the split twin, the driven pose, `@domain/retire`), and reopened and re-closed `B-44`; it added no
row, its residuals being listed in 16b section 9, row M, and its deferrals in section 4's cut M entry.
Cut AP's record (2026-10-02) closed two bugs (`B-86`, `B-87`) and built two features (`N19`, `N20`) on the
owner's rulings under `Q47` (a) and `Q57`; it added two owner questions (`Q58`, `Q59`) and four debts
(`T-100` to `T-103`), 103 bugs and 103 debts; its residuals are listed in 16b section 9, row AP, and its
deferrals in section 4's cut AP entry.

No P0 survives at `820ad9af`. The one leak-shaped item is `B-01`, which the verifier graded P1 because
what leaks is lobby roster state and not a position; it is first in cut D all the same. The live smoke
scanned 3,216 cockpit frames and found nothing about an undetected unit on any of them, and every unheld
designator (including a live contact's own sixteen-digit id read off the web payload) answers the one
byte-identical `No such contact.`

---

| B-84 — landed AI.1 | The reflex controller wastes its wake on two path searches that go nowhere. (1) Orbiting: a follower steers at an adjacent route cell inside its own turning circle, so its heading falls about thirty degrees a decide tick at 5 to 8 m/s, and the `follow` wandered-off branch (cut Q.7) clears the route and sets `searched_ms = None`, so a 7,000 to 16,000-cell search runs every 1,000 ms for as long as the orbit lasts. (2) The corner cut: a unit stopped `impassable` on a corner cut (154 of the harness cohort's 160 stops) has its route cleared by `note_blocked`, and `path_with`'s shoulder rule skips impassable shoulders, so the identical 13,667-cell route is searched again every `repath_ms` without the unit moving. In play a wingman circles instead of arriving and a tank sits on a corner; on the harness the authored lane's per-wake p99 tracks `path_cells` (r 0.77) and reads 8 to 17 ms by seed (2350: 11.6; 2351: 16.0 with a per-window p99 of 27.4 past the 20 ms lens; 2353: 13.3 before cut AE), so the 12 ms per-wake lens holds at the harness seed by chance. Moves the CI deterministic block (a bless with the reason) and the hash. | L | plausible (the sweep repairer's instrumented diagnosis at seed 2350 and a four-seed sweep; not yet reproduced in play) |
| B-86 — landed AP.1 | A pilot who fires the instant the gun is truly ready is told `Still cycling: 1 s to go.` and must type the line again: the cockpit's pre-check reads the mirror's `ready` flag and `ready_in_ms` exactly as the last frame carried them, nothing records when that frame was built and nothing ages it, and a frame is never re-sent for a countdown change, so the seat can fire only after seeing the ready frame, which adds a pulse (250 to 500 ms) to every cycle. Measured 219/2/45/39 pre-check refusals per run against 1/0/0/2 from the arena: nearly every `Still cycling` line a pilot reads is the mirror's, not the arena's. | B-76 — landed AA.1 | From the 2026-09-22 review: a reflex-flown tank whose engine is destroyed (`disable_motion`, struck through `hull.rear` on the stock `line_tank`; `arena_story.rs` calls an engine kill without a wreck the routine end of a rear-quarter duel) while it holds a classified hostile between `engage_cm` and `release_cm` (420 m to 600 m under doctrine or `far`; 280 to 400 under `effective`; 140 to 200 under `close`) never fires again. `Approach` enters `Engage` only inside `engage_cm` and reads no motion fact, so every decide tick it re-plans a stand-off cell it cannot drive to and emits Heading and Speed intents that are refused `MotionDisabled`; `engage_orders` is never called, although its own ceiling is `release_cm` and `resolve_fire` has no arc or motion gate, so the intact cannon may legally shoot at that range. Carrying a weapon it is never `disabled`, so it neither shoots back nor lets its side be eliminated. Health after the engine and the rear armour is 909 of 1000, above `withdraw_permille`; `Withdraw` would not rescue it either, since `withdraw_stand` fires out to `stand_cm` (200 m) until `arrived`, which a machine that cannot move never is. A healthy tank closing through the same annulus also holds fire, which 16b's cut E row defers on purpose; immobility is what makes the window permanent. ---

## 3. New functionality

The engine is ahead of the surfaces on it. The black box records everything a debrief needs and no verb
reads it; the order pipeline is complete and only a switched-off LLM can issue one; four key spots are
authored, digested and read by nothing; W8's `command.max_span` exists only in prose; the centre caps at
four a side on content while the config asks for twelve and the harness carries 300 units. So the top of
the ranking is mostly a surface for something built. Ranking is by what it gives a pilot in a fight per
line of work, with the scale target's one blocker (`N5`) placed where its dependents need it.

### N1. Name the ground — landed V.1

- **Pitch.** `The Ford`, `The Crossroads`, `North Barn` and `East Compound` go on the map, on `BEARING`
  and `RANGE`, on `STATUS` and into orders, so two pilots can say where they are in words.
- **Grounding.** `tacsim/content/arenas/proving_ground.json` authors four `key_spots`; they are typed
  (`tacsim:definitions/arena.rs:78-85`, cap 32), carried into arena state (`arena:state.rs:463`) and
  folded into the definition digest (`:520-521`). No behavioural consumer exists. Doc 16 §3.2 rules the
  ground surveyed: known to every pilot at check-out, its units not.
- **Sketch.** Carry the spots on `arena.map` and the map payload. `VIEW` marks those in the window with a
  letter and names them in a legend beside the grid (never in it, or the three renderers stop matching
  byte for byte), and names those just outside with a bearing. `BEARING <spot>` and `RANGE <spot>` are a
  second resolution arm that answers plainly; a name that is neither spot nor held contact keeps the
  byte-identical refusal. `STATUS` names the nearest spot.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** S. **Risk.** The parity fixtures.
- **Owner's choices.** Legend or in-grid names (default: legend plus a one-letter marker). May a pilot
  name a spot they cannot see (default: yes, surveyed ground). More spots now (default: no).

### N2. The debrief: `SIM REPORT`, `SIM HISTORY`, `@arena/report` — landed V.2 and V.3; the hit rate landed O.3

- **Pitch.** A match ends in one line. This gives each pilot a report of their own match (shots, hits,
  what they killed, what killed them, how long) and the centre a history of its last matches.
- **Grounding.** `write_result` (`tacsim:match_/record.rs:374`) writes one `tacsim:match:<id>` document
  per match and only tests read it (`mushd/tests/arena_story.rs:1351`, `sim_solo.rs:545`). The ledger
  (`arena:ledger.rs:70-318`) is appended to `log:match.<id>` (`tacsim:arena_bridge.rs:1293`, `:1306`) and
  nothing reads it. Four crates already page a ledger log through `mush_core::read_cold_range`
  (`mush-core/src/ledger.rs:996`, with `recent_sealed` at `:821` and `typed_entries` at `:196`). W16 calls
  the match ledger an in-world artifact.
- **Sketch.** `tacsim:match_/report.rs` and two arms on `Selector::ALL` (`sim.rs:99-107`), both gated by
  `lobby::outside_centre`. `SIM HISTORY` reads result documents only. `SIM REPORT [<match>]` pages the log
  with the visited bound the precedents use and folds `LedgerEvent` through one pure function (unit-tested
  against a harness match with no world). The projection is the design: a player's report carries their
  own `fired`, `hit` and `miss` lines, the hits on their own unit, the public outcome and per-side losses,
  and nothing of the other side's ammunition, movement or detections. Staff read the whole through
  `@arena/report`. A match whose tape has been archived says so.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** M. **Risk.** A report that starts as a ledger dump leaks; it is built
  as a projection from its first commit and the observer-rule test grows a report column.
- **Owner's choices.** What a player may read (default: their own projection plus the public outcome;
  staff the whole). Does the fog lift after an OOC match (default: no). History depth (default: 20).
  A panel feed (default: line text only).

### N3. `ORDER`: commanding the AI wingmen, and W8's knob — landed Y.1 and Y.2

- **Pitch.** `ORDER red-1 ENGAGE BA`, `ORDER ALL HOLD`, `ORDER red-2 WITHDRAW`, `ORDER red-1 ADVANCE The
  Ford`: one pilot and three machines become a lance.
- **Grounding.** `Objective` has four arms (`tacsim:deliberation/order.rs:66-88`) and two constraints
  (`:187-193`); `apply_intent` takes an order latest-wins (`arena:intents.rs:420-432`); `send_order`
  offers it (`tacsim:arena_bridge.rs:2130-2159`); `squads_of` cuts pod pairs (`deliberation/squad.rs:134-172`).
  The only issuer is the LLM seam, floored at 30 s, and the shipped server has `[llm].enabled = false`.
  Doc 07 §2's first case is the commanded wingman. `command.max_span` appears in doc 16 line 59 and 16a
  line 887 and nowhere in `crates/`.
- **Sketch.** A cockpit verb staged as `IntentKind::Order` through `forward_intent`, which already checks
  focus, the live `helm` grant and the epoch. Add `issuer: Option<ObjId>` to `Order` and resolve a target
  hint against the issuer's contacts first (`arena:reflex.rs:1330-1337` resolves against the squad
  commander today, and a pilot's tank is in no squad); `best_hostile` still chooses only among contacts
  the receiving unit holds, so a commander may point and a tank that cannot see still cannot see.
  `[tacsim.command].max_span` (default 4 squads per commander, with a per-minute order floor), validated
  by name at boot and asserted by the harness, as W8 says. A squad the pilot has no authority over takes
  the one byte-identical refusal. The acknowledgement says what the order means, because an ordered
  `WITHDRAW` still stands and fights for three ticks unless it carries `hold_fire`. `FOLLOW` is a fifth
  arm and a follow-on.
- **Depends on.** N1 for `ADVANCE <spot>`; cut E for machines worth ordering. **Size.** M.
  **Risk.** `Order` and `Objective` are hashed; the fixtures re-bless in one commit.
- **Owner's choices.** Who may order (default: the side's lead, and any pilot their own squad). The span
  (default 4). Does a human order outrank a live LLM order (default: yes, latest-wins). A radio line
  (default: no).

### N4. The reclaim — landed Y.3 and Y.4

- **Pitch.** A pilot whose link drops past the grace takes the tank back from the machine, with a report
  of what happened while they were gone.
- **Grounding.** The arena returns a unit on `Control { Live }` (`arena:intents.rs:358-365`); the world
  makes it one-way, because `Slot::left` is written from every route to `Released`
  (`tacsim:match_/lobby.rs:77-89`). Doc 07 §1.3 specifies the catch-up report. The smoke confirmed that
  revival inside the grace already works.
- **Sketch.** Split `Slot::left` into `left` (a deliberate `EXIT`, still one-way) and `dropped_at`. A
  `BOARD` from a dropped slot's account in a `Running` match re-runs the F.1 seat path and sends `Live`
  at a new epoch. `SIM STATUS` reads `flown by doctrine`. The report is built from arena state (contacts
  new and lost, sections against a small `at_release` snapshot, rounds spent, the controller's state and
  goal), rendered as one `Sys` block and carried on the frame as `catchup`. Doc 07 §1.4's invariants are
  assertions; `reclaim_min_ms` closes flapping.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** M. **Risk.** A reclaim racing a `Released` in flight; the epoch
  table settles it.
- **Owner's choices.** `EXIT` stays one-way (default: yes). The tank comes back where it is (default:
  yes). The report is projected through the observer rule (default: yes).

### N5. The authored reach fits the pulse — landed J.1, J.2 and J.3 (steps 1 and 2; step 3, the work budget, not built: `Q26` answered by the owner on the bounds arm, J.3b)

- **Pitch.** The answer to `T-34`, the choice carried for four cuts: make the trace cheap before choosing
  between the reach and the bound.
- **Grounding.** `walk` allocates three vectors per call and by its own comment never returns early
  (`mush-atlas/src/trace.rs:359-459`, `:415-416`); the arena reads only `sight` and `visited`
  (`arena:perception.rs:980-987`, `:1191`). Arena terrain is immutable for a match
  (`arena:state.rs:76-111`). A pass is already a timestamped due event.
- **Sketch.** Three steps, each measured on `T-35`'s cohort at 60 and 30. (1) `sight_only`: shares
  `line_cells` and the per-cell rules, allocates nothing and breaks at the first blocker; proved by a
  differential test over every ordered cell pair of the shipped ground. (2) `arena:horizon.rs`: a
  per-match max-top and any-opaque pyramid, conservative in both directions, that proves a line clear or
  blocked without walking it and descends on partial opacity; a property test and byte-identical
  `TACSIM_ASSERT` ledgers. (3) Only if the honest number is still over: a work budget per substep,
  nearest candidates first, the remainder resumed as a due event whose cursor is hashed.
- **Depends on.** `B-56` (the canonical walk is what the differential test is against), `T-35`, `T-22`.
  **Size.** M, then L, then L. **Risk.** Step 3 is the first thing in the arena whose outcome depends on
  load; the cursor is state, hashed and driven by arena time, and it is built only on an owner's word.
- **Owner's choices.** Which gives: the 600 m sight, the 4,000-trace bound, or the 300-unit target
  (default: build steps 1 and 2, re-measure, and only then choose; if still over, step 3 keeps both).
  RAM per arena for the pyramid (default: accepted; `T-36`'s memory bound covers it).

### N6. Lances — landed Z.1 and Z.2

- **Pitch.** Squads of four with two more mechanical orders, so that twelve machines a side is an
  opponent and not twenty-four duellists.
- **Grounding.** `SQUAD_SIZE = 2` (`tacsim:deliberation/squad.rs:40`); the reflex machine has no notion
  of a neighbour; `BriefContact` carries no condition (`deliberation/request.rs`).
- **Sketch.** `[tacsim.arena].squad_size` (default 4). Two `Objective` arms the reflex machine can obey
  from state it already has: `Screen { sector }` (hold an arc, fight what enters, do not pursue) and
  `Support { of }` (stay within a range of a named unit and prefer its target). The brief gains the
  squad's own picture. A 12 v 12 harness cohort with the LLM stubbed asserts orders per commander per
  minute under `max_span` and p99 unchanged.
- **Depends on.** N3, `T-35`. **Size.** L. **Risk.** A doctrine vocabulary the owner does not want; two
  arms, then ask again.
- **Owner's choices.** Lance size (default 4). Whether these are the right two (default: ship them and
  watch them fight). May an LLM command in a match with humans (default: yes, doctrine is the fallback).

### N7. Six a side — landed Z.3

- **Pitch.** The cap is content: eight pods and eight tanks. Raise the centre toward the size the engine
  carries.
- **Grounding.** `slots_per_side` clamps to `PODS_PER_SIDE = 4` (`tacsim:match_/centre.rs:60`) while the
  config default is 12; `POD_ENTRIES` is a compile-time table (`centre.rs:66-75`); the arena pack authors
  eight tanks; `MAX_ARENA_UNITS` is 24. Callsigns (bijective base 26), `ai_side_size` and the squad cut
  all scale already.
- **Sketch.** The OOC pack authors the extra pods in the two team rooms, `POD_ENTRIES` follows, the
  arena pack authors the extra tanks on the ford road muster. Two pack versions with migration chains;
  the stock digests move.
- **Depends on.** N3 (machines with no commander are noise at this size) and `T-36`'s fan-out bound.
  **Size.** M. **Risk.** The item budget of the OOC pack was not read; 12 real cockpit frames a side
  through the world's output path has no number until `T-36`.
- **Owner's choices.** Six or twelve (default: six first; twelve is one more bump once the fan-out has a
  number). New rooms (default: no).

### N8. A side channel — landed Z.4

- **Pitch.** Each side's pilots share a channel for the match, aliased by callsign.
- **Grounding.** `mush_comsys::api::force_join` and `force_leave` are public
  (`mush-comsys/src/api.rs:65`, `:122`) and comsys is first in tacsim's composition. `docs/help/cockpit.md:59-60`
  already says a callsign on the radio is a person you can look up; there is no radio.
- **Sketch.** `force_join` at check-out, `force_leave` in the release sweep and on `EXIT`. Machines do
  not speak.
- **Depends on.** Lands with N7. **Size.** S. **Risk.** A jammed mesh and an unjammed voice is a fiction
  decision and is taken on purpose.
- **Owner's choices.** Jammed by ECM (default: no, revisit for IC play). Alias (default: callsign). A
  cross-side channel (default: no).

### N9. Replay — landed I.1 and I.2

- **Pitch.** Watch a finished match from its black box.
- **Grounding.** `PositionSummary` names every unit every `ledger.summary_ms` (`arena:ledger.rs:103-106`)
  and `on_chunk` already parses it (`tacsim:arena_bridge.rs:1306`); with `arena.map`'s terrain and the
  shot events that is a whole replay with no seam change.
- **Sketch.** `SIM REPLAY <match>` over N2's reader, stepping keyframes on the one map-window rule; a
  scrubbing screen on the TUI and the web. Interpolation between summaries five seconds apart is a
  renderer's drawing and is labelled as such.
- **Depends on.** N2, N1. **Size.** L. **Risk.** A replay is as public as its projection rule.
- **Owner's choices.** Is a finished OOC match's replay public and objective (default: yes; the tanks are
  back at spec and it is practice, and this is deliberately the opposite of N2's default, because a
  replay is sat down to and a report is handed over). `ledger.summary_ms` (default: leave 5,000).

### N10. The referee's levers — landed I.3 and I.4

- **Pitch.** Staff can start, drive, abort and reset a match and can see nothing while it runs.
- **Grounding.** Five switches (`tacsim:match_/verbs.rs:48-58`); `MatchMirror` holds tempo, time and
  counts and no positions (`tacsim:arena_bridge.rs:399-414`).
- **Sketch.** `@arena/tempo <match>=<period_ms>`, a floor under the adaptive tempo (rate independence
  makes slow motion a supported state; a true pause is a fourth clock state and is not built).
  `@arena/place <unit>=<x>,<y>`, refused with an impact scheduled against the unit, re-running the cell
  entry test, journalled as its own kind and hashed. `@arena/watch <match>`: an `Observer` arm on the
  `Frames` message, a type distinct from `Frame` with no path into a pilot feed, pinned by a contract
  test, built only after `T-36` bounds the fan-out.
- **Depends on.** N9 for the screen, `T-36`. **Size.** M. **Risk.** `@arena/place` writes a pose outside
  the motion path.
- **Owner's choices.** Pause or slow (default: slow). `@arena/place` at all (default: yes, in `Running`,
  nothing in flight). Announced in-world (default: journalled always, announced never).

### N11. `SIM MODE`: Hold — landed O.1

- **Pitch.** A side that keeps a named key spot uncontested for N seconds of arena time wins.
- **Grounding.** `eliminated` is the only positive win (`arena:damage.rs:1086-1105`); `EndReason` and
  `MatchOutcome` are closed enums (`tacsim:match_/record.rs:105-171`), which doc 16 §7 says is where a
  new arm goes.
- **Sketch.** A `SIM MODE` selector in `Selection`, announced like `SIM UNIT`; `modes: []` on `ArenaDef`
  so a pack declares what its ground supports. `Hold` is one range test per unit per decide tick against
  at most 32 cells. The objective is settled before elimination and `spent` after it. The AI fill takes
  `Objective::Advance` to the spot as its default goal, which is N3's machinery used by the arena on
  itself. `Defend` and `Escort` follow.
- **Depends on.** N1, N3. **Size.** L. **Risk.** A wide shallow diff through every `EndReason` consumer;
  the compiler finds it. A §4 amendment.
- **Owner's choices.** First mode (default: `Hold`). Lobby choice or arena property (default: both).
  Does elimination still end it (default: yes, settled second). Public in selection (default: yes).

### N12. `EMCON` — landed O.2

- **Pitch.** A tank carrying a pod jams its own side with no switch.
- **Grounding.** Doc 16 §11 X.4 and Q.6: powered pods within their radius of the observer fold into
  detection and the mesh; `line_tank_ecm` is fieldable; `tacsim.power` is the precedent for a toggled
  component state.
- **Sketch.** A `tacsim.ecm` capability state, `EMCON on|off` as a discrete intent on `Channel::Sensors`,
  one clause in the two jamming folds, one ledger kind, one notice that says what switching off costs,
  one `STATUS` line. The stock digests do not move. Posture (hull-down scaling the presented height) is
  the follow-on and is not scheduled: it moves the hit curve for every shot and every hash.
- **Depends on.** `B-22` for the words. **Size.** S–M. **Risk.** Low.
- **Owner's choices.** Default state at check-out (default: on, as today).

### N13. The range — landed O.3

- **Pitch.** Targets that do not shoot back, and a match that does not end when one is hit.
- **Grounding.** An unarmed stationary unit is not `out_of_action` until wrecked
  (`arena:damage.rs:1053-1067`); `spent` excludes an unarmed side; `refit_unit` can field a type on a
  machine slot, which the lobby cannot ask for today.
- **Sketch.** `target_mk1` in the fielded catalog (the stock sections, no weapon, zero speed) and
  `SIM RANGE [<n>]`, which fields targets on the machine slots. Ends when every target is wrecked or on
  `SIM ABORT`; N2's report gives the hit rate. Check by test that a `UnitSpec` with no weapon survives
  `create_unit`, `refit_unit` and check-out.
- **Depends on.** N2. **Size.** M. **Risk.** Low, mostly content.
- **Owner's choices.** A mode of the centre or a room (default: a mode). Still or shuffling (default:
  still).

### N14. `@macro` — landed AD.1

- **Pitch.** `@macro l=lock $1`. Platform-wide, most useful in a cockpit whose action verbs are typed on
  every surface by design.
- **Grounding.** `AliasStage` and `add_alias_stage` exist (`mush-core/src/cmd.rs:377`, `:626`). A narrow
  grep found no player-facing alias verb; if one exists under another name this is smaller.
- **Sketch.** `@macro <name>=<line>`, read and `/del`; one expansion only (a macro that expands to a
  macro is refused); `$1` to `$3`; never over a registered verb; 20 per player.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** M. **Risk.** A macro must never reach a verb its player could not
  type.
- **Owner's choices.** Platform-wide (default: yes). Positional arguments (default: three).

### N15. Content breadth, with the legged cost row — landed AE.1, AE.2 and AE.3

- **Pitch.** Six unit types and three weapons prove the mechanisms. A scout, a heavy, a second biped, an
  autocannon, a missile with a flight time and one capped drone make a catalog.
- **Grounding.** `tacsim/content/` and the extension tables at `tacsim:definitions/content.rs:162-267`;
  W2 and `arena.max_units_by_class`; `B-58`, `T-15`, `T-18`.
- **Sketch.** Three tranches. Chassis, which pays `B-58` and `T-15` and authors `T-18`'s bluff. Weapons,
  which makes `FIRE`'s default weapon a choice. One drone under W2's cap. No infantry: at 10 m cells with
  one unit per cell the model has nothing to say about a person.
- **Depends on.** `T-35`. **Size.** M and L. **Risk.** Low; definitions are versioned with migration
  chains.
- **Owner's choices.** Infantry ever (default: no). Drones as content (default: one, capped).

### N16. Per-pulse durability and resume — landed AF.1, AF.2 and AF.3

- **Pitch.** Doc 16 §11's first "Before IC play" item: a restart stops voiding the match.
- **Grounding.** D-16-3. `Arena` and `UnitState` derive `Clone, Debug` only (`arena:state.rs:1015`,
  `:2282`); the ack-gated ledger lane exists (`tacsim:arena_bridge.rs:281-343`); every substep has a
  state hash; `write_restart_abort` is what would stop voiding (`tacsim:match_/record.rs:450`).
- **Sketch.** `Serialize` on the state tree behind a versioned `snapshot` module whose deserialised
  `state_hash` must equal the recorded one; the definition and terrain are re-derived from the digest.
  An `arena -> world Snapshot { at_ms, hash, bytes }` message every `arena.snapshot_ms`, written on the
  arena writer lane with disjoint key ownership. Boot re-creates the arena from the last snapshot at or
  after the last acked chunk, replays the ledger tail, and re-opens the task; a hash mismatch voids
  loudly. Pilots return through N4. The arena still holds no store handle.
- **Depends on.** N4; cut M's hash and timestamp work. **Size.** XL. **Risk.** A snapshot that diverges
  silently; the test snapshots every pulse of a scripted match and asserts a byte-identical ledger from
  any resume point.
- **Owner's choices.** Cadence (default: every 5,000 ms plus tail replay, not every pulse). Do OOC
  matches resume (default: yes, it is the cheapest proof). Re-read `deliberation/mod.rs:54-88` when this
  lands, because its second argument stops being true.

### N17. `VIEW BRIEF` — landed AG.1

- **Pitch.** The frame in sentences, for a screen reader and for a pilot who would rather read.
- **Sketch.** The ground on eight bearings, run-length described, then the contacts as `CONTACTS` words
  them, then the key spots. A second renderer of one frame; nothing new crosses the seam.
- **Depends on.** N1. **Size.** M. **Owner's choices.** Build it before a player needs it (default: yes,
  small, after the fighting features).

### N18. First-time hints — landed AH.1

- **Pitch.** One sentence naming the next verb at five points, once per account, `@tutorial off`.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** S. **Owner's choices.** Now or with the second player (default: with
  the second player; `T-77`'s two help sentences are the part worth doing now).

### N19. `WATCH`: a pushed digest for line mode — landed AP.3

- **Pitch.** A line pilot keeps a picture of the fight without typing for it: one to three lines pushed
  on every changed frame, the way screen 0 redraws itself on the TUI and the web.
- **Grounding.** Line mode gets notices only: the bridge pushes an `arena.cockpit` keyframe on every
  frame change (`tacsim:arena_bridge.rs`'s `on_frames`, `tacsim:match_/cockpit.rs`'s `say_and_frame`),
  but `mush-ansi` renders a `Panel` only with a grid shape (`mush-ansi/src/render.rs`'s `Panel` arm), so
  position, speed, heading, `HELD`, weapon readiness, contact ranges and the lock's chance reach a line
  pilot only by typing `CONTACTS`, `STATUS`, `WEAPONSTATUS` or `RANGE`. The bot spent 60 to 84 per cent
  of its lines on reads, three in five returning nothing new; a human at a terminal cannot keep a picture
  at all; and the help never says that line mode gets no self-updating picture. Verified at `f0e6ef95`.
- **Sketch.** A cockpit verb `WATCH ON` and `WATCH OFF` (a per-pilot flag on `ArenaRegistry`). While it
  is on, `on_frames` pushes, after the keyframe it already sends on change, one to three `Sys` lines from
  that same frame: own (power, kph, heading, `HELD`), weapons (ready or cycling per gun, the lock with
  its range and per cent to hit), contacts (designator, bearing, band, range, age). A pure function of
  the pilot's own frame, so the observer rule is untouched; no tempo word (`T-59`); one extra `Sys` per
  watching pilot per changed frame, counted against `Q-U2`'s 512 bound (288 measured at 24 pilots); each
  line within 120 columns. One help paragraph on what line mode pushes; the digest pinned in a cockpit
  unit test and in the bot's `arena_contract.rs`. The server half only.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** M. **Owner's choices.** Where the digest is made (default:
  world-side, from the pilot's own frame, built in AP.3; the heavier gateway-side alternative is an
  opt-in `#frame arena.cockpit 1 {json}` rendering in `mush-ansi`). On by default (default: no, a pilot
  types `WATCH ON`, so human text is unchanged).

### N20. Reply framing for line mode: `OUTPUTPREFIX` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX` — landed AP.4

- **Pitch.** A scripted line client, the bot or a human's triggers, knows where each reply begins and
  ends without inferring it, the way PennMUSH clients have for decades.
- **Grounding.** A read reply is a run of `Sys` lines with no count, closing line or correlation id
  (`ClientCommand::Req` and `OutputEvent::Reply{id}` correlate only structured panel queries; there is no
  `OUTPUTPREFIX` anywhere); `VIEW BRIEF`'s empty contacts slot shares the `No contacts.` constant with
  `CONTACTS`; a look, an arrival, a teleport and the hatch eject emit the identical `RoomView`;
  `@arena/pose` answers success and refusals as unrelated plain sentences. Inferring where replies end
  cost the bot cut AM, and it still misattributes on a slow wire. Notices do not land inside a reply: a
  command's synchronous reply is queued contiguously in one world step (at most 9 intrusions in about
  19,400); the ambiguity is where a reply ends and which pipelined line it answers.
  (`mush-server/src/world_task.rs`'s dispatch; `tacsim:match_/cockpit.rs`'s `contacts`, `weapon_status`,
  `status`, `brief_lines` and `NO_CONTACTS_LINE`; `tacsim:match_/verbs.rs`'s `pose`;
  `mush-core/src/commands/mod.rs`'s `show_room`.) Verified at `f0e6ef95`.
- **Sketch.** PennMUSH-style per-connection `OUTPUTPREFIX` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX`, set by a world command and
  stored per connection: the world pushes the prefix before dispatching a typed line and the suffix after
  its synchronous output is queued, a line deferred behind a commit on B.5's path included, with
  unsolicited notices outside the brackets. Opt-in, so human text is unchanged and the AJ contract holds;
  the look-against-arrival ambiguity is subsumed by the brackets. Two riders that stand on their own:
  `VIEW BRIEF`'s empty contacts gets its own sentence (`No contacts in the window.`) and a fixed closing
  line, and `@arena/pose` refusals are wrapped as `POSE refused. {reason}`. World-side, a server-half
  restart; a `mushd` line-mode e2e and the help for the new command.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** M. **Owner's choices.** Opt-in per connection (default: yes, so the
  AJ contract and human text stay as they are). Retire the bot's reply-inference lane once it lands
  (default: yes, in a bot cut after AP, the bot keeping its inference for a server without the command).

### N21. Doctrine draws: the machines' opening stops being a fixed script

- **Pitch.** The same muster stops playing the same opening: a machine draws its patrol, its stand-off
  and its start from the match seed, and halts to look on the way, so a pilot cannot learn one script and
  a stopped pilot does not always see first.
- **Grounding.** Doctrine makes no random choice: `patrol_goal` picks `neighbours[id % len]`, the hunt
  cell and the stand-off are pure functions of the unit id, the ground and the plots, and the seed
  reaches the machine only through `stagger_ms`'s sub-second phase and the hit, facet and burst draws. So
  from the shipped muster the opening repeats to within about 2 s (first contact 136 to 141 s) and 91 per
  cent of incoming hits sit in the 400 to 499 m bin; the machine never halts to look and never leaves the
  road graph on approach, so against a stopped pilot (seen at 480 m on grass while the mover shows to
  600) the pilot saw first 10 of 10 with an 8 to 13 s lead. The nose-on `Engage` is sound and stays.
  (`arena:reflex.rs`'s `patrol_goal`, `hunt_goal`, `far_entry_cell`, the `Patrol` and `Approach` arms and
  `Ranges`; `arena:rng.rs`'s `DrawLabel::LABEL_KINDS`, five kinds, and `stagger_ms`;
  `mush-contract-tests/tests/arena_library.rs`.) Verified at `f0e6ef95`.
- **Sketch.** `DrawLabel::Doctrine { unit, choice_seq }`, keyed by unit and decision ordinal (never a
  tick, so cross-tempo holds; a sixth label kind is a reviewed contract-test diff), used in
  `patrol_goal`, once at adoption for a per-unit stand-off offset inside the band (about ±40 m on
  `engage_cm`) and for an optional start delay; a bound-and-halt in `Patrol` and the hunt (speed 0 for
  `[tacsim.arena.reflex].halt_ms` on a waypoint's arrival, one new hashed `ReflexUnit` stamp, the halts
  issued as intents so `it can do nothing a pilot cannot` holds); harness assertions over several seeds
  on the first-contact spread and on an engagement-range histogram not in one 100 m bin. It moves
  `state_hash` (a new row field), the reflex cross-tempo and E.4 timing tests, the harness baseline,
  `help simulator`'s machine paragraph and doc 16 §3.5 and §6; the same seed is still the same match. The
  server half.
- **Depends on.** Nothing. **Size.** M. **Owner's choices.** The offset's width (default: about ±40 m on
  `engage_cm`) and the halt's length (default: one `halt_ms`, set on the harness spread). Whether it
  waits for the unit minds (no default: the owner's unit-minds decision of 2026-10-02 puts the AI-filled
  machines on one mind code path, where a doctrine draw would live;
  `docs/design/proposals/unit-minds.md`, being written).

### N22. Debrief counters

- **Pitch.** The engine measures its own matches: a pilot's report says where it was hit, when it saw and
  was seen, how often it was held and refused; staff read per-unit and per-commander counters and a
  history aggregate, so nobody has to instrument a client to know how a match went.
- **Grounding.** The engine already writes `tacsim:match:<id>`, a per-pilot report and a public replay,
  and the staff pager prints every tape event, but nothing summarises: the pilot timeline is capped at
  `REPORT_MAX_LINES` 200, so a match with about 400 hits taken loses its facet split; there are no
  first-contact, first-shot or first-hit-taken times, no held count, and no refusals at all (both `FIRE`
  halves are world-side and never reach the tape); nothing reports the commander's objective mix, reply
  age, shield drops or changed-objective share (the bot found its own 65 per cent withdraw rate and 80
  per cent unchanged holds by instrumenting itself; the engine's commander is unmeasured); there is no
  machine shots-per-second figure; and `SIM HISTORY` is a 20-row ring with no aggregate. Every
  measurement the bot cuts built (Wilson intervals, pooled runs, facet splits) was built outside the
  engine, from transcripts. (`tacsim:match_/report.rs`'s `Fold::absorb_pilot`, `header_lines` and
  `absorb_staff`; `tacsim:match_/record.rs`'s `result_document`, `UnitResult`, `history_lines` and
  `HISTORY_DEPTH`; `tacsim:match_/cockpit.rs`'s `Cycling` pre-check and `refusal_line`;
  `tacsim:arena_bridge.rs`'s `MatchDeliberation`, `deliberation_counts` and the `@arena/status` orders
  line; `tacsim:deliberation/cost.rs`'s `CostRow`.) Verified at `f0e6ef95`.
- **Sketch.** Untruncated, serde-default `Fold` fields counted as the events pass (pure, `BTreeMap`, no
  float): for the pilot `Hits taken: front 13, left 152, right 74, rear 149.`, `First contact 2 m 17 s; first shot 2 m 20 s; first hit taken 2 m 31 s.`
  and `Held 4 times.`, plus a per-unit refusal tally on `MatchRecord` from the cockpit path (`FIRE refused 9 times: 7 still cycling, 2 no lock.`);
  for staff per unit, the first detection each way, hits dealt and taken by facet, refusals, the
  machine's shots per second and the hit exchange from `shots_fired` and `hits` (W.2);
  `MatchDeliberation` counters per objective word, shield and stale drops, the maximum reply age and the
  changed-objective count, on the `@arena/status` orders line and as cost-table columns with latency p50
  and p95 from the audit rows; a staff `@arena/history [n]` folding the result documents (wins per side,
  timeouts, aborts, mean and median duration, per mode); keys on the result and report documents. No new
  `EventKind`s, no hash; `LIVE_FORMAT` unbumped; the server half.
- **Depends on.** N2 (landed V.2 and V.3). **Size.** M. **Owner's choices.** What a player reads
  (default: their own counters; hits dealt by facet and who saw whom first are staff-only, by `Q37`, N2
  and `the fog does not lift`). Whether the commander's counters come before `Q53` is ruled (default:
  yes, `Q53` measures first).

### 3.1 Not before a design conversation

| Id | Proposal | What the conversation must settle |
|---|---|---|
| H1 | The first IC fight: an arena on real ground with units that belong to somebody. An arena can already stand on a real atlas region, `@arena/start` already runs the lifecycle over arbitrary units, and check-in already commits a wreck and spent rounds. A `TacticalUnit` has no owner and the match lifecycle has no edge to `mush-economy`. | Is a destroyed unit lost or a repair bill (doc 10 §10 says meaningful loss softened by insurance). Who may start a fight. Proving ground with owned units first, or real ground. Whether the ownership layer lands here or in its own conversation. N16 is arguably a prerequisite. |
| H2 | Wrecks and salvage: a second `SalvageSource` beside `SimSalvageSource` (`tacsim:composition/salvage_source.rs:19-60`). | Who may claim a wreck they did not own; hangs off H1 and the ownership layer. |
| H3 | Rearm and repair with a price: two verbs on `refit_unit`'s governed write and the first consumer of the ten commodity families. | Instant or timed; anywhere or at a facility; hangs off H1. The free OOC path and the priced IC path are one lane with two policies. |
| H4 | The 10 km arena. The grid takes it; four budgets sized against 256 do not, and 1M cells of authored relief is about 2.8 MB against a 1 MiB pack cap, so the relief must be generated. | 10 m or 25 m cells (25 m silently changes every tuned fight). Whether it is wanted before a fight that needs the room. |
| H5 | Live spectating for players, including a dead pilot watching their own side's shared picture. | Does a spectator see the world or a projection. The strict reading is replay only, which N9 already gives. |
| H6 | A perception lens on `match_object`. | This is the Epistemic Engine's kernel seam (observer-dependent identity); that conversation shapes it. `T-67`'s audit gate is the part built now. |

---

## 4. Execution order

Fourteen serial cuts and a tail. Ledger letters used before this document: R G P H A T C K S W N X F Q;
the twelve below use the rest of the alphabet, and the review cut AA (after E) opens the second series, the owner's ruling cut AB (after Y) follows it, and the owner's fix cut AC (after I, before the 2026-09-26 dev1 redeploy) follows that; the tail's five cuts AD to AH (after O, started on the owner's word of 2026-09-27) close the second series, with the fix cut AI (`B-84`, from cut AE's sweep) placed after AF, and the owner's bot cut AJ (the external bot flies a pod, 2026-09-29) after AI, and its follow-up AK (the bot finishes what it starts, 2026-09-30) after AJ, then AL (the bot turns into the shot) after AK, then AM (the bot holds up on a real box) after AL, then AN (the bot fights from the muster) after AM, then AO (the bot keeps its back off the wall) after AN, then AP (the engine takes the bot's lessons, from the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) after AO.
Each cut is a functional boundary of roughly cut F's size: three to five sub-cuts of 1.5k to 3k lines
with their tests. 16b §0's build discipline applies unchanged: the workspace green at every sub-cut, the
gates re-blessed in the commit that moves them, a reviewer that did not write the cut, one commit and one
push per sub-cut, a ledger row in 16b §9 per cut, and doc 16 §11 amended in the cut that makes a sentence
of it untrue. A cut that moves `state_hash` re-blesses the cross-tempo fixtures once, in its last
sub-cut. "Gateway" below means the deploy needs a full restart of the gateway half and not a server-half
reload, because the TUI, the session layer or the embedded web bundle changed.

Landed 2026-09-29 at `28fcdaf1`; see 16b section 9, row AJ. Deferred, none of them a 16c item: the pilot's tuning the win-rate reading names (fall back at the sight's edge, the held state at 0 kph, about half the `FIRE` lines refused as still cycling) and the machine side of it (Q-AJ5, a later machine cut), the lead's live smoke with the real key (stale answers at the default routing, the real cost a match, a drop-and-reboard), and the parser, lobby, commander and test residuals the AJ row's Deviations cell writes out.

Added 2026-09-29 on the owner's word ("give it a goal of defeating the blue team in the arena simulator"), after AH and AI, with the owner's three choices of the same day: a hybrid brain (a fast deterministic pilot, an optional LLM commander above it), either role (alone against the machines, or a wingman beside a human on the same side), and a lettered cut.

**When it lands.** `mush-bot --arena red` (or `/arena red` at its prompt) walks from Limbo to the centre, claims a slot, leads the side if nobody does, boards, starts the tank and fights the match to its end from the line-mode terminal alone; it re-fires every weapon the moment its cycle ends, keeps moving, and works for the flank or the rear of whatever it has locked; an optional model re-sets its doctrine about once a second and at once on events; the report says who won.

- **AJ.1** The picture: a `mush_bot::arena` module that reads every line a seated line-mode pilot can get (the `arena-notice` lines, `CONTACTS`, `RANGE`, `BEARING`, `WEAPONSTATUS`, `STATUS`, the lobby's and the centre's lines, `Match N is over (<word>)`) into one `Picture`: the own tank (power, speed, heading, position, sections), the contacts (designator, side, bearing, range, plot age, a position fixed from the own position, successive plots giving an estimated course), the weapons with their cycle clocks, the lock, and the match phase with its result word. Offline golden tests, and a format-contract test (dev-dependency only) that feeds the game's own formatters' output through the parser so a changed line breaks a test and not a match.
- **AJ.2** The pilot: a deterministic tactician over the `Picture` and a pipelined arena lane in the runner beside the activity path (no settle window per line, a rate of at most 4 lines a second under the session's 10, polls sized to the pulse's tempo). The lobby flow as a small state machine (lead when first on the side; never `SIM START`, `SIM AI` or `SIM CONCEDE` under a human lead). Fire: each ready weapon in range, the lock on the identified hostile with the best odds. Movement: hold a band outside the machine's stand-off, orbit on the tangent with reversals so its plots go stale, steer for its side or rear from its estimated course, fall back when the own hull is failing. Outcome: the result word for the own side sets `done` and `success`, the other side or a draw sets `blocked` with the reason; `--matches n` for a rematch loop. Written from player-visible text only: nothing of `tacsim::arena::reflex` is read or copied (crates/mush-bot/AGENTS.md).
- **AJ.3** The commander: when a model is configured, a separate small prompt every `commander_ms` (1,500, never more than one call in flight) and at once on events (a contact identified, a section lost, a lock broken for long) gets a compact picture and returns a `Doctrine` (`target`, `stance` of orbit, close, stand-off or withdraw, `band_m`, orbit sense, `note`), validated and clamped, the pilot's defaults when there is no model, the model tape and the budget as for any other call; a stale answer (older than two pulses of the picture it was asked about) is dropped. The bot's `[model]` gains two pass-through keys the client does not send today, `reasoning` (OpenRouter's object, e.g. `{ enabled = false }`) and `provider` (e.g. `{ order = ["Together"], allow_fallbacks = false }`), validated as objects and recorded in the request fingerprint. The example config names `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` with reasoning off. The goal loop gains an `arena` action so a plain `--goal` ("beat the blue team in the simulator") can hand the seated pod to the pilot.
- **AJ.4** The proof: an end-to-end test in `crates/mush-bot/tests` that boots a `mushd` world with the centre (dev-dependency), launches the real bot binary with `--arena red --headless` and no model against one blue machine, and asserts a finished match with hits scored and an honest report; a wingman test with a human on red beside the bot; a commander test on a model tape; a report-only win-rate lane (`--ignored`) over ten seeded matches at 1 v 1 whose result is recorded in the AJ row; `docs/bot.md` gains an "Arena pilot" section and `bots/arena/` an example config.

Measured before the cut (2026-09-29, owner's ask; a 1.2 KB picture and a five-field doctrine, about 37 output tokens): `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` through OpenRouter with reasoning off answered in p50 1,342 ms on the default routing (313 to 1,597 ms, 8 of 8 valid, about $0.00008 a call) and p50 333 ms pinned to Together (295 to 479 ms, 10 of 10 valid, about $0.00017 a call); with `reasoning: {effort: low}` it took p50 10.6 s (up to 56.8 s) and five of eight answers spent the whole 1,500-token budget thinking and returned nothing. So the model is quick enough to re-plan faster than the machines decide (1,000 ms) but never quick enough to pull the trigger: the pilot keeps the lock, the fire cadence and the steering, and the commander sets where it goes and what it hunts. A 5-minute match at one call every 1.5 s is about 200 calls, about three US cents pinned.

Depends on nothing of the simulator's (the bot reads the shipped text). Gateway: no. Packs: none. Hash: no. Server code: none expected; a test-support helper in `mushd` at most. Size: medium to large, four sub-cuts.

### Cut AK: the bot finishes what it starts

Landed 2026-09-30 at `618b616a`; see 16b section 9, row AK. Built as the three sub-cuts below, each committed and pushed on its own green gate and a ship-with-notes verdict (AK.1 `d380c268`, AK.2 `f843d974`, AK.3 `618b616a`), then the full sweep in one round with no repair (6,530 passed, 1 failed and 88 ignored on the workspace, the one failure `mushd` `observer_rule`'s TUI cockpit test reading a partial paint under load and passing alone on its first rerun; 473 passed and 2 ignored on `mush-bot`; the weekly harness green with the deterministic block equal on 109 fields, so no baseline row is added); the entry cites no `B-nn`, `T-nn` or `N-n` row, so none closes; `Q-AK1` to `Q-AK5` assumed at their defaults (section 5); the no-model figure on the 420 s cap is 4 of 10, selected from three runs of 2, 4 and 4, against the report-only target of seven; no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved, and the commander's out-of-sight band is clamped to 450 m rather than refused. Deferred, none of them a 16c item: the lead's one live commander run (flown 2026-09-30 after the record: 1 of 5 won, 3 lost, 1 timed out, 418 calls, 417 answered, none late, 0.0626 US dollars; read in cut AL's entry), and the tail the runs found for the next bot cut (a one-track tank turning toward its dead side, the fall back a no-op at the default 450 m band, a held-then-search loop into one slope, `FIRE` typed for guns lost with the turret), with the pilot, commander and rig residuals the AK row's Deviations cell writes out.

Added 2026-09-30 on the owner's word ("run AK and keep chasing tail items as they appear"), from the AJ win-rate reading and the lead's live commander smoke.

**Why.** AJ's ten-match lane (no model, 1 v 1, tanks posed 500 m apart, the lane's own 180 s match cap) read 4 won, 1 lost, 5 timed out; every timeout was one-sided (38 to 65 hits scored against 34 to 48 taken, no kill), because a machine that is losing falls back out of the 600 m sight and the pilot does not follow it. The lead's live smoke on 2026-09-29 (the same rig, five matches, `bots/arena/commander.toml`'s real `deepseek/deepseek-v4.1-flash` pinned to Together) read 1 won, 1 lost, 3 timed out; 370 commander calls, 368 answered, 1 late, 2 transport errors, 0.0706 US dollars in all (about 1.4 cents a match), about 410 ms a call; and the commander chose `withdraw` for 238 of its 367 doctrines (65 per cent), most of them on a lost contact ("no contacts and lock lost ... withdraw to 900 m to rebuild the picture"), which sends the tank away from the fight it is winning. AJ's residuals add the pilot's own causes: the fall-back band equals the sight's edge, a held state that stays held at 0 kph, about 46 per cent of `FIRE` lines refused as still cycling, a fall back read from any facet of any section, and a `withdraw` doctrine with no time bound that outlives a commander that stopped.

**When it lands.** A bot that is winning finishes the match: it follows a contact it lost to the last plot and along its estimated course, sweeps when the trail runs out, and re-engages; it falls back only on its own failing hull and never past its sight; it types a `FIRE` only when its own clock says the weapon is ready; the commander hunts rather than withdraws on a lost contact; and the ten-match figure is re-measured on a cap long enough to tell a win from a timeout.

- **AK.1** The pilot finishes: a hunt state (lost contact: drive to the last plot, then along the estimated course, then a bounded sweep, back to engage on the first fresh plot; never past the arena's edge, a `Held:` line ends a leg), the fall-back band held inside the sight with margin and fall back read from the struck facets of the own hull only, the held-at-0-kph state released, and fire lines scheduled from each weapon's own cycle clock (the `Still cycling: N s to go` refusal and `WEAPONSTATUS` both feed it) so a refused `FIRE` is the exception; offline tests for each on the `Picture` and the tactician.
- **AK.2** The commander hunts: the doctrine gains a `hunt` stance; `withdraw` expires (a bounded hold, then the pilot's default) and is cleared when the commander stops for any reason; the commander's system prompt is rewritten from the measured failure (a lost contact is a reason to hunt, a withdrawal is for a failing hull, the band stays inside the sight), with the prompt's guidance pinned by an offline test over recorded pictures; the doctrine clamp refuses a band beyond the sight.
- **AK.3** The re-measure: the win-rate lane's match cap raised so a finished fight is not cut off (default below), the lane made to finish its report when the bot ends early, and an opt-in live commander lane (ignored, run only when `MUSH_BOT_LIVE_COMMANDER=1` and `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` are both set, never by CI) that flies five matches with `bots/arena/commander.toml`'s model block and prints its figure, calls, late answers and cost; the builder runs the no-model lane (at most three tuning rounds of AK.1's constants, each run and its figure recorded) and the lead runs the live lane once after the cut.

Depends on AJ. Server, arena, packs, gateway and `web/`: none (crates/mush-bot/AGENTS.md holds; the tactics stay the bot's own, written from what a pilot reads, never from `reflex`). Hash: no. Size: medium, three sub-cuts.

### Cut AL: the bot turns into the shot

Landed 2026-09-30 at `9f56cb9d`; see 16b section 9, row AL. Built as the three sub-cuts below, each committed and pushed on its own green gate and a ship-with-notes verdict (AL.1 `778fbb12`, AL.2 `1427d0cd`, AL.3 `9f56cb9d`), then the full sweep in one round with no repair (6,530 passed, 1 failed and 88 ignored on the workspace, the one failure `mush-server`'s `world_task` test of what still waits when the loop ends, the known `Closed` and `CommitReceipt` `select!` race, passing alone on its first rerun; 535 passed and 2 ignored on `mush-bot`; the weekly harness green with the deterministic block equal on 109 fields, so no baseline row is added); the entry cites no `B-nn`, `T-nn` or `N-n` row, so none closes, and the AK.3 pilot residuals it names (the one-track circle, the fall back that showed the flank, the held-then-search slope loop, `FIRE` for a lost gun) are closed by its code; `Q-AL1` to `Q-AL4` assumed at their defaults (section 5), `Q-AL2`'s bound on the 500 ms pulse rig loosened by the programme lead's ruling of 2026-09-30; the hold read 5, 5 and 4 of 10 on the 420 s cap (4 of 10 selected on the guns' refusals) and a forced orbit 4 of 10, against the report-only target of seven; per `Q-AL3` the AL.3 bullet's per-ground ceilings are not built, the pilot stopping wherever it stands and reading no ground; no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved. Deferred, none of them a 16c item: the lead's one live commander run on the new policy, a live measure of the ambush wait and of AL.3's two review fixes (no run reached them), a ruling on the moved one-track lane test, the scripted reply-order root cause behind `a_crowded_minute`'s early hurt mark, and the cadence, nose-on and seen-second residuals the AL row's Deviations cell writes out. The programme lead's rulings on AL's open items (2026-09-30): AL.3's change to `a_one_track_tank_never_circles_for_a_small_turn` (BA's second bearing taken from the tank's mid-turn heading plus 170 degrees, with its preconditions made real checks and every later assertion unchanged) is accepted as a repair of the test's set-up, not a weakening; the head-on lane is a 50-50 duel by construction (both tanks posed 500 m apart, inside each other's sight, so the help's edges of being seen second and of the machine's documented habits never come into play: no ambush wait fired in 40 matches), which cut AN measures from the muster. The lead's live commander lane on AL (2026-09-30, `plan-data/cut-AN/live-commander-al/`): 4 of 5 won, 1 lost, none timed out; 267 calls, 264 answered, 1 late, 1 error, 0.0427 US dollars; applied doctrines 100 `hunt`, 82 `orbit`, 57 `withdraw`, 20 `close`, 5 `hold`; hits taken 130 of 183 on a front facet; the first lane on which the bot beat the machine more often than not, on five matches.

Added 2026-09-30 under the owner's standing word of the same day ("keep chasing tail items as they appear"), from the lead's reading of AK.3's kept transcripts.

**Why.** AK's hunt turned AJ's one-sided timeouts into fights, and the bot lost them: the three ten-match runs on the 420 s cap read 2, 4 and 4 wins with five losses each, the lost matches over in 60 to 120 s with the bot out-hit about two to one (27 hits scored against 63 taken in 81 s; 32 against 90 in 119 s). The lead's count over run 2's transcript (`plan-data/cut-AL/ak3-run2-reading.txt`) names two causes, both against the help. First, **the bot fires at half its guns' rate**: the lane types 2.2 lines a second of its 4, but the machine gun's `FIRE` lines went a median 2,055 ms apart (p10 1,649, p90 2,850) against a 1,000 ms cycle and the cannon's 7,250 ms apart (p10 6,796) against 6,000, with only 14 `Still cycling` refusals in ten matches, so the schedule's slack, not the budget, is the limit. Second, **the orbit hands the machine the bot's flank**: of the hits the bot took, 177 landed on the hull's side facets, 124 on the turret's and 119 on a track's side facet, against 23 on a front facet; `help cockpit` says a side is twice the target (7 m against 3.5 m of bow), carries 250 against the front's 400 (the turret 220 against 350), and puts a track at risk three times in ten, and ends "Turn into the shot." The AJ entry's premise that moving spoils the machine's aim was the lead's and was wrong: a cannon round crosses 450 m in under half a second, and in run 2 every one of the bot's own 113 misses read `the round went wide` and none `the target moved`, though the machine it shot at moved to close. The lead's live commander lane on AK (2026-09-30, `plan-data/cut-AL/live-commander-ak/`) reads the same: 1 won, 3 lost, 1 timed out; 418 calls, 417 answered, none late, 0.0626 US dollars; the applied doctrines 199 `hunt`, 117 `orbit`, 101 `withdraw` (so AK.2's prompt worked), and still 32 of the 494 hits taken on a front facet, 76 on a rear one, the machine gun's `FIRE` gap a median 2,154 ms and the cannon's 7,350 ms: the commander cannot fix what the pilot does under it. AK.3 adds four pilot residuals on the same paths: a tank with one track gone turns toward its dead side and circles (every hunting timeout was such a tank), the fall back is a no-op at the default 450 m band and leaves the damaged flank toward the machine, a held-then-search loop drives into the same slope eight to ten times, and after the turret is destroyed the pilot keeps typing `FIRE` for guns it no longer has.

**When it lands.** The bot fights nose-on and at its guns' full rate: it stops and turns its bow to the hostile it is fighting, opens and closes range by driving along the line of fire (reversing to open it) rather than across it, turns into a shot that lands on a side, fires each gun on that gun's cycle with the slack of one pulse and no more, stops typing `FIRE` for a gun it has lost, and stops before it is found where the help says a stopped tank is seen closer in; the commander's stances and prompt say the same; and the ten-match lane is re-measured with the old orbit beside the new default.

- **AL.1** Full cadence: each gun's `FIRE` is scheduled from its own cycle clock with at most one engaged pulse of slack and takes the line budget ahead of any poll that can wait; `WEAPONSTATUS` polling thins once a gun's cycle is learned; a gun named in `That unit carries no weapon called ...` or lost with its section is struck from the schedule for the match; an offline lane test asserts the machine gun's median `FIRE` gap is at most 1,200 ms and the cannon's at most 6,500 ms over a scripted engagement, with `Still cycling` refusals at most one line in ten.
- **AL.2** Nose-on: the pilot's default engagement is a `hold` that stops at the band and keeps the bow within 30 degrees of the hostile's bearing (help: within 45 the front takes the round); range is changed along the line of fire, forward to close and in reverse to open, so the fall back is a reverse with the bow kept on; a hit notice on a side or rear facet turns the bow toward the bearing of the hostile that fired; a tank with a track destroyed turns only the live way (or reverses to turn) and the hunt aims its legs to suit; a `Held:` stop marks that heading and cell so the search never drives the same slope twice; `orbit` stays a stance but is no longer the default; offline tests on the tactician for each.
- **AL.3** Seen second, and the re-measure: while searching and when a hostile is plotted or expected inside the sight, the pilot stops before it is found (the help's per-ground ceilings, read from what the bot can see: its own STATUS and the contact rows, never the map's hidden terrain) and lets the mover come to it; the commander gains `hold`, loses the moving-spoils-aim premise, and is told the help's facet and sight rules; the win-rate lane runs the new default up to three times (tuning only AL's named constants) and once with `orbit` forced, every figure and the facet split of hits taken recorded; the lead runs the live commander lane once after the cut.

Depends on AK. Server, arena, packs, gateway and `web/`: none (crates/mush-bot/AGENTS.md holds; every rule above is the player help's, never `reflex`'s). Hash: no. Size: medium, three sub-cuts. AJ's robustness residuals (reply fencing for a remote box, the stop races, the room-heading race, `/send` and `/pause`, the unexpected `STARTUP` sentence, the channel-continuation hole, the runner-level commander test, the wingman mark race, `docs/bot.md`'s internal ids) stay with the next tail cut.

### Cut AM: the bot holds up on a real box

Landed 2026-10-01 at `36ecdc90`; see 16b section 9, row AM. Built as the three sub-cuts below, each committed and pushed on its own green gate and a ship-with-notes verdict (AM.1 `493ae8b1`, AM.2 `f0a59529`, AM.3 `36ecdc90`), then the full sweep in one round with no repair (6,530 passed, 1 failed and 88 ignored on the workspace, the one failure `mush-gateway`'s `server_panic_relaunch` test of an in-game shutdown, whose forked server sent no hello inside its timeout under load, passing alone on its first rerun; 587 passed and 2 ignored on `mush-bot`; the weekly harness green with the deterministic block equal on 109 fields, so no baseline row is added); the entry cites no `B-nn`, `T-nn` or `N-n` row, so none closes; `Q-AM1` to `Q-AM3` assumed at their defaults (section 5), the latency rig's mean round trip on its seed 821 ms rather than the plan's about 380, on which neither gun's cycle is learned; no server, arena, pack, gateway or `web/` source moved, no tactic changed and no win rate measured. Two corrections to this section's sketch are recorded in the AM row: line mode prints no prompt in play, so a reply closes at the first line after it that is not one of its rows and not chatter, a hint or a receiver notice (never at a prompt-shaped line); and `/send` still pauses the bot (a person typing takes the controls), what changed being that a pending command never holds the lane's tick. Deferred: AM.3's `a_crowded_minute` item (`CONTACTS` rendered at the scripted server's delivery, `an_identification_never_starts_a_hunt` and the hurt mark taken out), blocked on a lead's ruling on `a_due_gun_keeps_its_slot_from_a_poll`'s `held_back > 0` and saved as a patch that applies at `36ecdc90` — landed AN.1 (the minute re-phased by `CROWDED_NOTICE_PHASE_MS` 600, no assertion re-blessed), with the reply, ledger, lobby, pause and test residuals the AM row's Deviations cell writes out. The programme lead's rulings on AM's open items (2026-10-01): AM.1's continuation rule as built (the echo's chain kept, a leading-space line after a full-width line read as a continuation, every column-0 `[` line read as chat before the hint and transient checks) is accepted, since it errs toward never swallowing an expected answer; AM.3's unfinished item, `a_crowded_minute` without its hurt mark under `CONTACTS` rendered at delivery, is to be finished by re-phasing that minute so a `STATUS` falls due while a gun is due, never by re-blessing `a_due_gun_keeps_its_slot_from_a_poll`'s `held_back > 0` (the assertion is that test's point), and is carried into AN.1; the latency rig's 821 ms mean round trip leaves both guns' cycles unlearned (the machine gun 19 shots a minute against about 55 on loopback), which is recorded and not chased while the plan's measured round trip to the dev box is 35 to 48 ms; AM.2's remaining correctness notes (a pause that spans a whole boarding window and the start, forty busy `STARTUP` answers in a row, the paused branch the runner never reaches, the description-line race on a round trip over about 1.5 s) and the user guide's last internal party wait for the next robustness tail.

Added 2026-09-30 under the owner's standing word of the same day ("keep chasing tail items as they appear"): the robustness residuals AJ, AK and AL each set aside for "the next tail cut", gathered into one.

**Why.** Every bot lane so far has flown on loopback against a rig in the same process, where a reply arrives in one piece inside a few milliseconds. The owner will fly it against a server across the internet, and the cuts' judges named what breaks there or under a person's hand on the REPL: a `CONTACTS`, `WEAPONSTATUS` or `STATUS` reply split by a gap of more than 150 ms or by an interleaved line closes early, and the whole-list rule then drops contacts and their fix history or clears a held lock (AJ.1 R7); a padded channel or speech line can put a game sentence at the start of a continuation line (AJ.1); `close()` decides on `SIM LEAVE` from a snapshot, so a `SIM TEAM` or `SIM START` in flight at a stop can leave the bot holding, or leading, a slot (AJ.2 F7); the lobby's look and walk steps act on any room heading, not only their own answer (AJ.2 F6); a `STARTUP` answered with a sentence the step does not expect blocks after its timeout and loses the centre's real result word (AJ.2); a `/send` during a fight stalls the lane for up to `observation_ms` and a `/pause` can end in a blocker on `/resume` (AJ.2 F9); a multi-match run that blocks reports the last flown match's sentence and not the blocker's (AJ.2 F8); a `FIRE` answered by a transient line holds that gun for two seconds (AL.1 R5); the pilot's wait and unidentified-stop clocks go stale while the tank is immobile (AL.3); and the tests the judges found thin: no runner-level test of the commander's wiring (AJ.3), the wingman's `Good hunting` mark race (AJ.4), the win-rate rig's refusal list missing `There is no slot ...` and its `Client::send` panic through `try_pose` (AK.3), and `a_crowded_minute`'s scripted reply ordering, which marks a machine hurt before it is sighted to hide a momentary hunt (AL.3). `docs/bot.md` also cites internal ids (`Q-AJ2` and the like) a user cannot look up.

**When it lands.** The bot reads a reply that arrives in pieces, late, or with someone else's line in the middle of it the same as one that arrives whole; nothing typed on a channel can be read as a game sentence; a stop never leaves a centre held or led; every lobby step acts only on its own answer and knows every sentence the game can give it; the REPL's `/send`, `/pause` and `/resume` never stall or block a flight; a multi-match report says why it stopped; and a latency rig proves it with delays and split writes like a distant box's.

- **AM.1** Replies on a slow wire: a reply is closed by its own structure (the line after the last row it can hold, or the next prompt-shaped line), never by a quiet gap alone; an interleaved notice or channel line is read as itself and the reply goes on; a line that follows a full-width channel or speech line is a continuation, never a game sentence; a transient answer to `FIRE` re-arms that gun at once; a scripted-server latency rig (one-way delays and jitter up to 300 ms, replies split across writes, a notice dropped into the middle of a reply) on which the picture, the lock and the fire clock hold, beside the existing loopback tests.
- **AM.2** The lobby and the REPL: a stop with a claim or a start in flight waits for its answer (bounded) and then leaves; each look and walk step accepts only the answer to its own line; the `STARTUP` step knows every sentence the cockpit can answer it with (a destroyed tank, power already up, not seated) and a match keeps the centre's result word whatever the step did; the lane ticks while a `/send` is pending, and `/resume` restarts the lane's deadlines from the resume instant; a blocked multi-match run reports the blocker's sentence with the tally; the pilot's wait clocks advance while the tank is immobile.
- **AM.3** The thin tests: the commander's wiring through the runner on the SSH rig with an HTTP stub (readiness, budget, cancel, the transcript records, model calls never counted as goal calls), the wingman's mark taken before `BOARD`, the win-rate rig's refusal list and its `try_pose` failing rather than panicking, `a_crowded_minute` fixed at the scripted server's reply order rather than by marking a machine hurt it has not seen, and `docs/bot.md` written without internal ids.

Depends on AL. Server, arena, packs, gateway and `web/`: none. Hash: no. Size: medium, three sub-cuts.

Added 2026-09-30 under the owner's standing word of the same day, from the lead's reading of AL.3's runs and `help simulator`'s "Fighting the machines"; queued after AM.

**Why.** AL made the bot fight nose-on at nearly its guns' rate, and its lane reads it: every hit taken in the two `hold` runs the lead read landed on a front facet (hull front 205 and turret front 150; 235 and 169), the machine gun's accepted shots went a median 1,498 ms apart (from 2,154 before AL) and the cannon's 6,550 ms (from 7,350), and no match timed out; the figure is 5, 5 and 4 of 10, against 4 of 10 with `orbit` forced. What is left is a fair duel: the lane poses both tanks 500 m apart, inside each other's sight, so every match is two identical tanks nose-on from the first second, and the machine's gun still runs a little faster than a line-mode pilot can type (one shot a second against about 1,250 ms on the arena's 250 ms lattice). The match the owner plays starts at the muster, 2,430 m apart, and `help simulator` tells any pilot how the machines play it: a machine patrols, closes to about 420 m when it sees you, stops, turns onto you and shoots; open the range past its sight and it comes after you again; let the ground hide you and it drives at where it last saw you; one that has seen nobody for a minute and a half crosses to the other side's entry zone to look; hurt badly enough it breaks off for its own entry zone, fighting on the way only what is inside about 200 m, shoots back at whatever comes for it once home, and after twenty quiet seconds comes back in, once. `help cockpit` adds that a stopped tank on grass is seen out to 480 m and a moving one to 600. Each of those is an edge a pilot who waits can take and none of them can happen on the posed lane (no ambush wait fired in AL.3's 40 matches).

**When it lands.** A second win-rate lane flies from the shipped muster with no pose and is the figure of record; the bot opens by holding stopped near its own entry, bow toward where the machine will come from, and takes the first shots at a mover it sees before it is seen; when a stopped machine is winning the exchange it opens the range past the machine's sight, stops, and takes the next approach the same way; it follows a machine that has broken off from outside the range the help says it fights at, into its rear, and waits outside its sight for the one return; and the machine gun fires on the arena's lattice at the line-mode floor.

- **AN.1** The muster lane: the win-rate lane flown from the muster with no pose on a 600 s cap (beside the posed lane, kept as the duel benchmark), its reading lines adding who saw whom first (the first contact line against the first hit taken), the opening's length and the share of hits taken from the front; one run at the AM default as the baseline.
- **AN.2** The opening and the re-approach: hold stopped near the own entry with the bow toward the other side's entry until a hostile is plotted (the bearing read from what a pilot can see: its own `STATUS`, `VIEW`'s window and named spots, the contact rows), then fire from the halt; open the range past the machine's sight when the exchange (hits scored against taken over a window) runs against the bot, stop, and fire on its approach; each behind an `[arena]` knob so the lane can compare.
- **AN.3** The broken machine and the lattice: a machine seen driving away after hits is followed at a band outside the help's 200 m and inside the bot's sight, fired at from behind, and not followed into its entry zone; the bot then waits outside the machine's sight for the one return; the machine gun's schedule learns the arena's 250 ms lattice from its own acknowledgements and fires at the floor; the muster lane run up to three times (tuning AN's named constants) and once with each AN knob off, every figure recorded.

Depends on AM. Server, arena, packs, gateway and `web/`: none; every rule is the player help's (`help simulator`'s "Fighting the machines", `help cockpit`'s sight and facet rules), never `reflex`'s. Hash: no. Size: medium, three sub-cuts.
