### Cut AO: the bot keeps its back off the wall

Landed 2026-10-02 at `3ca72512`; see 16b section 9, row AO. Built as the three sub-cuts below, each committed and pushed on its own green gate and a ship-with-notes verdict (AO.1 `78a14526`, AO.2 `5394a17f`, AO.3 `3ca72512`), then the full sweep in one round with no repair (6,530 passed, 1 failed and 88 ignored on the workspace, the one failure `observer_rule`'s TUI cockpit test, a load flake that passed alone on rerun; 686 passed and 4 ignored on `mush-bot`; the weekly harness green with the deterministic block equal on 109 fields, so no baseline row is added); the entry cites no `B-nn`, `T-nn` or `N-n` row, so none closes in sections 1 to 3, and it closes its own items, AN's residuals it named and AM.2's four REPL notes; `Q-AO1` to `Q-AO3` assumed at their defaults (section 5), and `Q-AN3`'s wingman half now keeps its opening; no server, arena, pack, gateway, `web/` or harness source moved. Measured from the muster (report-only, local, 2026-10-02): three default runs 2, 4 and 6 of 10, pooled 12 of 30 (95 per cent Wilson 25 to 58); the re-approach off 3 of 10 and the lattice off 3 of 10; by `Q-AO1`'s rule the re-approach ships off (it reached its wait in 11 of 31 back-offs) and the lattice stays on (6.9 against 15.9 per cent of `FIRE` lines refused at an equal shot gap); the sixth run at that default 3 of 10, the posed duel 8 of 10. Two corrections to this section's sketch are recorded in the AO row: a back-off cannot turn along the edge with its bow kept on, so it slants up to 30 degrees or ends and holds, and a paused start cannot re-board a match whose slot the game dropped, so that match is read as lost and the next claimed. Deferred, for a later cut: the commander's policy still naming the re-approach the shipped `commander.toml` turns off, the move in paid for the follow alone at the default, the live commander lane's figure without an interval, the follow knob never flown off, the known REPL end-to-end flake, and the pilot, lobby, pool and test residuals the AO row's Deviations cell writes out.

Added 2026-10-01 under the owner's standing word of 2026-09-30 ("keep chasing tail items as they appear"), from the lead's reading of AN.3's runs and the residuals AM and AN left.

**Why.** AN's opening works: from the muster the bot saw the machine first and shot first in all fifty matches flown, its lead before the first hit taken grew from about 8 s to 12 or 13 s, and every hit it took landed on a front facet. The figure moved with it but not cleanly: the muster lane read 4 of 10 at AM's pilot, then 7, 5 and 2 of 10 at AN's default and 8 of 10 with the re-approach off. Two causes are in the transcripts. First, **the bot backs into its own wall**: the opening holds 15 to 67 m from the west edge, so the re-approach (begun 6 to 9 times a run) reverses into the edge and never opens the range to its 650 m wait, and every return wait began on its time bound for the same reason; the one run without the re-approach won 8. Second, **a single run of ten is noise at this figure**: three runs of one build read 7, 5 and 2, so no knob can be chosen on one run. AN also left: the lattice clock built but not effective live (its guard's bound, 150 to 200 ms, sits under the measured acceptance edge of about 250 ms, so the machine gun was refused about one line in five against AL's one in seven, at the same 1,500 ms shot gap), and a `ready` row still pulling the lattice clock; a hurt machine that publishes no track as it leaves is never judged broken; `broken()` trusting an implausible track (181 m/s); a followed row 3 to 6 s old under a `hunt` doctrine neither flown nor hunted; the return's back keeping its bow on a stale plot when the machine reappears; and a wingman at the muster losing its opening to its teammate's return. AM left four robustness notes: a pause that spans a whole boarding window and the start ends the run on `BOARD`; forty busy `STARTUP` answers give a running match up; the lane's paused branch is one the runner never reaches; a foreign description line can still answer the walk on a round trip over about 1.5 s; and the user guide still names "the owner's target".

**When it lands.** The bot holds its opening with room behind it, and a back-off that meets the edge turns along it rather than into it; a hurt machine leaving without a track is followed; the lattice either beats the plain clock live or is off by default; the wingman keeps its opening; a pause, a busy start and a slow walk never end a run; and every knob's default is chosen on pooled runs with their interval stated.

- **AO.1** Off the wall: the opening's hold is chosen with at least the longest back-off the bot can order (from about 420 m to 650 m of a plot) of passable ground behind it (on the side away from the other entry, along the line between the two entries), read from `VIEW BRIEF`'s header and ground (stepping inward toward the other entry when the muster has none); a back-off that is held or comes within a margin of the edge turns along the edge with the bow kept on, or ends and holds; the return's back follows a fresh plot; `broken()` also judges a hurt machine whose range opens with no track, and rejects a track faster than the help's stock tank can drive with a margin; one stale limit for a followed row under every doctrine; a wingman's opening ignores returns at the muster within a teammate's distance.
- **AO.2** The lattice and the REPL: the lattice guard's bound widened to admit the measured edge and the `ready` row's pull fixed, the lattice left on by default only if AO.3 shows it refusing fewer lines at an equal or better shot gap; a pause through a boarding window and the start re-boards the running match (built instead as that match lost, said and recorded, and the next claimed, since the game drops a slot never boarded; see the AO row); a busy `STARTUP` never gives a running match up; the runner ticks the lane while paused so it reads and settles; the walk takes only its own room's heading and description; the user guide's last internal party removed.
- **AO.3** The pooled measure: the muster lane flown three times at AO's default and pooled (thirty matches), each knob still in question flown once off, the posed lane once, every figure printed with a 95 per cent Wilson interval beside it, and the knobs' defaults set by the written rule of Q-AO1 in the commit that measures them; the lead's live commander lane from the muster after the cut.

Depends on AN. Server, arena, packs, gateway and `web/`: none; every rule is the player help's or the bot's own transcripts', never `reflex`'s. Hash: no. Size: medium, three sub-cuts.

### Cut AP: the engine takes the bot's lessons

Landed 2026-10-02 at `ea3a2624`; see 16b section 9, row AP. Built as the four sub-cuts below, each committed and pushed on its own green gate and a ship-with-notes verdict (AP.1 `790b9dc9`, AP.2 `901b7563`, AP.3 `5a2312ba`, AP.4 `ea3a2624`), then the full sweep in one round with no repair (6,555 passed, 2 failed and 88 ignored on the workspace minus mush-bot, both failures load flakes green alone on their first rerun: `observer_rule`'s TUI cockpit test, on the known list, and `persistence`'s `m5_module_persistence_round_trip_in_memory`, not on it, `T-103`; mush-bot 689 passed; web 558 of 558; the harness 7 of 7 lanes with the delta inside 20 per cent and the deterministic block equal; no hand-played match on this build). Deferred: `Q47` (b), the deadline tie-break; the wake pull-in for a ready `FIRE` (`Q58`) and the aged figures on the re-pushed keyframe (`Q59`), both defaults not built; `B-88`'s disarmed notice, now owed for the multi-unit side alone, and `B-99`'s refusal; the `hold_ms`-past-the-cap check and a ceiling on `max_duration_ms` (`T-100`); an outcome word for a disarmed hull (`T-101`); the brackets across a hot restart and on the lines the gateway answers (`T-102`); the bot's reply-inference lane, to retire in a bot cut after AP; and dev1's `mushd.toml`, which keeps 3600000 until the owner edits it and restarts the server half.

Added 2026-10-02 on the owner's word ("yes, fold them into 16c and write up the P1 cut .. 900s timeout is fine"; the arena being "an explicitly OOC construct", a proving ground for testing the rest of what is built), from the engine-lessons sweep of the same day over the six bot cuts AJ to AO, with the owner's rulings of the same day on `Q45`, `Q47` and `Q57` (section 5).

**Why.** Six cuts built an external line-mode pilot that knows nothing of the engine but the player help, and everything it had to measure, guess, retry or build a heuristic for is a property of the engine. The sweep (2026-10-02, read-only at `f0e6ef95`) mined 121 findings from the six cuts' judge outputs, their transcripts and the help, verified 70 of them against the code, and merged them into 44 levers, four of them P1, now `B-86` to `B-103`, `T-90` to `T-99`, `N19` to `N22` and `Q45` to `Q57`. The four P1 levers are this cut. First, **the seat's own mirror refuses the shot** (`B-86`): nearly every `Still cycling` line a pilot reads is the cockpit's, not the arena's, 219/2/45/39 pre-check refusals per run against 1/0/0/2 arena-side, because the mirror's ready flag is whatever the last frame carried and is never aged, so a pilot can fire only after seeing the ready frame, a pulse (250 to 500 ms) late on every cycle; the bot built a whole slack-and-lattice apparatus around it, and a human simply types the line again. Second, **a match that is won does not end** (`B-87`): a turret hit empties the machine's weapons, it withdraws, never rallies and still moves, so it is never disabled and its side stands until the one-hour cap, recorded `timed out (the clock ran out)` with no winner and the help contradicted twice; most of the bot's recorded stalemates were this, hidden by its 420 s lane cap, and in the mirror case 34 of its 46 losses passed through about 50 s of a gunless tank being shot with no word that it was out of the fight (`B-88`). Third, **line mode is pull-only** (`N19`): position, speed, readiness and contact ranges reach a line pilot only by polling, so the bot spent 60 to 84 per cent of its typing on reads, three in five returning nothing new, and a human at a terminal cannot keep a picture at all. Fourth, **replies are unframed** (`N20`): no count, no closing line and no correlation id, so inferring where a reply ends cost the bot a whole cut (AM) and it still misattributes on a slow wire (notices do not land inside a reply, at most 9 in about 19,400; what is ambiguous is where a reply ends and which pipelined line it answers). The owner's rulings of 2026-10-02 shape the rest: the shipped `max_duration_ms` drops from 3,600 s to 900 s, the arena being an explicit OOC proving ground, and the damage tie-break at the deadline is not built (`Q47`); `B-87` applies to the pilot's side as well, and a pilot late to board gets `B-99`'s refusal and no takeover (`Q57`); and nothing fires for the pilot (`Q45`): the seat's cadence parity comes from the unit minds, the machines paying the seat's pulse, so this cut ages the mirror and builds no armed trigger.

**When it lands.** A `FIRE` typed the instant the gun is ready is taken, and `WEAPONSTATUS` counts down from the frame's receipt instead of repeating the last frame; a side with no gun left loses at the hit that disarms it, on either side, and no match runs past 900 s; a line pilot can type `WATCH ON` and read the fight in one to three pushed lines a changed frame; and a scripted client can bracket every reply with its own `OUTPUTPREFIX` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX`, with `VIEW BRIEF`'s empty contacts said in a sentence of its own before a closing line and `@arena/pose` refusals reading `POSE refused. {reason}`.

- **AP.1** `B-86`: `CockpitFrame` stamped with the `Frames` message's arena `now_ms` and the world `Instant` of its receipt; `fire()` and `WEAPONSTATUS` read the aged remainder (`ready_in_ms` minus the wall ms since receipt, skipped while a staff tempo floor stands), the pre-check forwarding at a remainder of 0 or less and the arena taking the `FIRE` at its next wake (as built, no cadence is claimed: an intent never pulls the wake in, so the sketch's consistent cycle + 250 ms is `Q58`); `WEAPONSTATUS` prints the aged remainder; cockpit tests that a `FIRE` typed after the true ready instant and before the ready frame is forwarded, and that a staff floor leaves the remainder unaged. No hash, no protocol, no sentence change.
- **AP.2** `B-87` with `Q57`'s symmetry and the 900 s cap: the side clause in `Arena::eliminated` beside AE.3's drone clause (a side whose every in-action unit was armed at check-out and now has `weapons.is_empty()` is out while some side still carries a weapon; both disarmed is the existing elimination draw; keyed on structural disarm, never on ammunition, so Q.7 stands), for both sides by D-16-5; `[tacsim.match].max_duration_ms` 900,000 at every default site (tacsim's `MatchConfig`, the `mush-config` default and its head table, `mushd.toml.example`, `deploy/instance/mushd.toml.tmpl`); `help simulator`'s end-of-match and machines sentences made true, and a new sentence there stating the 900 s cap (the help states no cap today, `T-92`); the code comments that call the cap an hour corrected (tacsim's `config.rs` beside `MatchConfig`, `arena/state.rs`, `arena/damage.rs`, and `mush-config`'s `config.rs`); doc 16 §3.8's elimination wording and its §11 sign-off line written out and declared for the committer; the `damage.rs` `eliminated` tests, the `step.rs` settle-order tests, the E.4 and `B-04` endgame cases, `match_lifecycle.rs`, the `mush-config` defaults test (its `(10_000, 120_000, 3_600_000, 12)` assertion) and the weekly harness baseline moved with the reason (field matches end earlier).
- **AP.3** `N19`: the cockpit verb `WATCH ON` and `WATCH OFF` (a per-pilot flag on `ArenaRegistry`); while it is on, `on_frames` pushes after the keyframe one to three `Sys` lines from the same frame (own, weapons, contacts), a pure function of the pilot's own frame with no tempo word, each line within 120 columns and one extra `Sys` per watching pilot per changed frame against `Q-U2`'s 512 bound; one help paragraph on what line mode pushes; the digest pinned in a cockpit unit test and the bot's `arena_contract.rs`.
- **AP.4** `N20`: per-connection `OUTPUTPREFIX` and `OUTPUTSUFFIX`, set by a world command, pushed before a typed line's dispatch and after its synchronous output is queued (a line deferred behind a commit on B.5's path included), notices outside the brackets, opt-in; and its two riders: `VIEW BRIEF`'s empty contacts gets its own sentence (`No contacts in the window.`) and a closing line, and `@arena/pose` refusals are wrapped as `POSE refused. {reason}`; a `mushd` line-mode e2e and the help for the new command.

Standing decisions, from the sweep's list as the owner's rulings leave it: section 6's `fire when ready` row stays (`Q45` ruled no auto-fire; the row gains a clause), and so does section 6's absolute substep lattice (`B-86` does not need it); doc 16 §3.8's elimination wording is amended in AP.2 with its §11 line (`B-87`, `Q57`); doc 16 §11's reader-contract bullets are amended for every sentence `B-88`, `B-89`, `B-92`, `B-94`, `N20` and `T-93` change, each a declared reader change with the bot's `arena_contract.rs` moving with it (in this cut, `N20`'s riders); and `Q-U1` is revisited only if `Q55` chooses (a) or (b).

From the 2026-10-02 engine sweep (`plan-data/engine-lessons-2026-10-02/`). Depends on nothing of the bot's code; queued after AO. Gateway: no (the server half only; `N20` is world-side). Packs: none. Hash: no. Help: yes (`help cockpit` and `help simulator`). The bot's `arena_contract.rs` moves with every sentence change, a declared reader change under doc 16 §11. Size: medium, four sub-cuts.

### The unit-minds programme

Decided 2026-10-02: docs/design/17-unit-minds.md (from the proposal docs/design/proposals/unit-minds.md, 1125c834, with the owner's rulings of the same day: the seal is per sortie, the OOC sim crosses no IC boundary and carries exactly the character's IC minds at no cost, only a character principal (human- or AI-played) may ORDER and a mind never, player minds fly NPC hulls under licence). Its build order M1a, M1b, M2 to M8 is doc 17 section 22; the cuts are ledgered in a 17b when the first launches; cut AP precedes M1a, which needs no Rhai.

## 5. Open questions for the owner

Each has a default, and the build proceeds on the default unless the owner says otherwise before the cut
that assumes it. Feature choices are listed with their feature in §3; the ones below belong to bugs and
debts, plus the four feature choices that change the most.

| Q | Question | Default | Cut |
|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | Is the enemy roster lobby knowledge that stays readable mid-match? | No: own side in full, the other side as callsigns alone, no `seated` or `left`; the callsign-to-slot mapping stays public. | D |
| Q2 | What does a destroyed pilot get for the rest of the match? | They stay seated, are told once what the options are, and read nothing new; a view of their side's mesh waits for H5. | D |
| Q3 | A match whose last human has left: finish, stop, or slow down? And may a side concede with a result? | `SIM CONCEDE` for the lead records a loss; an unattended match ends with its own reason after `match.unattended_ms` (300,000). | D |
| Q4 | May the cockpit write one optimistic fact into its own mirror? | Yes, one: a `Starting` hint that the next frame confirms or clears. D-16-1 stands for everything else. | D |
| Q5 | The web composer: shell-level, a cockpit-local row, or return-to-screen? | Shell-level, which is the TUI's shape. | B |
| Q6 | Does `look` learn the module namespaces, or does the atlas write a bare `detail:`? | `look` tries `detail:` and then each registered detail prefix; the namespace gate is untouched. | D |
| Q7 | Is a defer queue for player commands worth it? | Measure first; then depth 4, 250 ms, refused on timeout in a player's words. | B |
| Q8 | At what range and health does a hurt machine turn and fight? | Inside the fired weapon's close band only, and never below half of `withdraw_permille`. | E |
| Q9 | What does `Approach` do with range and no line? | It closes on the plot; the hold is the least of doctrine, order and current range, priced off the fired weapon's inner band. | E |
| Q10 | Does a refusal stop the tank (doc 16 §3.4 step 4) or may it slide? | Terrain, edge and slope stay literal; an occupancy hold keeps the speed. Sliding is not built. | E |
| Q11 | Is a wreck a physical fact the controller may read, or knowledge it must earn? | Knowledge: the contact's own reading, alive until the contact expires. | E |
| Q12 | A round on a destroyed section: absorbed, or transferred inward? | Absorbed and journalled as today, with its own rate-limited sentence. | D |
| Q13 | Is a zero-heat weapon refused at the platform's ceiling? | No. | D |
| Q14 | Short weapon names, or exact ids and a fixed help? | Unique prefixes and content-declared short names, printed on `WEAPONSTATUS`. | D |
| Q15 | Friendly fire: silent, warned or gated? | Warned, never gated. | D |
| Q16 | The silent rename: a new notice, or no hysteresis silence when the class regressed? | A notice; one line per treeline flicker. | D |
| Q17 | Does a platform know it is being jammed? | It knows its own receiver is degraded; it does not know who or from where. | D |
| Q18 | Does the `CONTACTS` band describe the weapon or the scope? | The lesser of the two; the weapon's own bands stay on `WEAPONSTATUS`. | D |
| Q19 | Is an authored band list a hard range limit? | Yes: past the last band a shot resolves blind. | M |
| Q20 | The TUI notices pane: filter the scrollback, or render the frame's notices? | Render the frame's notices; two screens may word one event. | B |
| Q21 | The cockpit tab's rule on both clients? | Seated-once. | B |
| Q22 | Move the `blocked` journal to the held edge and hash `held`? | Yes, with the cause on the event; every hash moves, in cut M with the rest. | M |
| Q23 | Make discrete actions rate-independent, or amend D-16-2? | Amend: a discrete action resolves at the end of the pulse that accepted it, and a production-shaped test says so. Reflex shots do become rate-independent (`T-02`). | M |
| Q24 | Build the absolute substep lattice? | No: amend §3.1 and assert that the fielded catalog's substep divides the three periods. Revisit with the first unit above 3,996 cm/s. | M |
| Q25 | May due events coalesce, amending §3.1 phase 5? | Yes, inside one substep length; and the harness measures the loop the task runs. | U |
| Q26 | Which gives: the 600 m sight, the 4,000-trace bound, or the 300-unit target? | None yet: build the cheap trace and the horizon index, re-measure, choose on that number; if still over, the work budget keeps both. Built to that default through J.3 (2026-09-25) and still over: after `sight_only` and the horizon index the authored lane's per-wake p99 is 7.699 to 8.156 ms and its per-window p99 14.108 to 15.092 ms over three weekly runs against the 12 ms lens (10.3 ms, 12.0 / 1.16, is what a weekly assertion needs), with 452 share operations a wake against a bound of 256; the lane stays report-only. The arms with their figures: shorten the sight (48 cells still over at 14.454 ms per window; 36 cells holds 12 at 11.234 but not 10.3, and needs `max_share_ops_per_pulse` at 768); move the bounds (768 for share operations, the lens still missed by 2.1 to 3.1 ms per window; holding the 16 per cent margin would need a 17.51 ms lens); lower the target (240 units holds 10.3 by 0.39 ms at 9.911 on one run with share operations to 512; 200 units holds every current bound at 9.478); or J.4, the per-substep trace budget with a hashed resume cursor, which keeps all three. Each sizing figure is one run inside the harness's 16 per cent noise; three confirming runs are owed before an arm is relied on. The builder would take the target arm; the owner decides. Answered by the owner on 2026-09-25 ("keep the authored reach and the 300 units, raise the bounds and set the lens with margin"), the bounds arm: keep the authored reach and the 300-unit target, `max_share_ops_per_pulse` 768, the authored lane's per-window lens 17.5 ms, asserted weekly by hand; built as J.3b (`664465ff`): first three asserted runs per window p99 15.339, 14.057 and 14.732 ms and per wake 8.099, 7.785 and 8.150 ms, every bound held (the gate's fourth run 16.298 and 8.377). The lens carries the 16 per cent margin over 15.092, the figure it was set on; over J.3's fourth run (15.807), J.3b's first (15.339) and the gate's fourth (16.298) it holds by 10.7, 14.1 and 7.4 per cent, so a loaded week can miss on noise: leaving it, adding a re-run rule, or setting 18.9 ms (16.298 × 1.16) is the owner's next call, and only the owner moves the lens. Ruled again later the same day: 20 ms ("bump the lens up to 20 to give us some headroom but keep a reasonable boundary"), built as J.3c (`036633d6`): 22.7 per cent over the worst asserted run on record (16.298), under the 25 ms `engaged_p99_ms` ceiling, pinned at the owner's figure by `the_lens_is_the_cohorts_own`; the confirming run read 15.732 ms. J.3d (`75309700`, 2026-09-29): the 16 per cent ratio check retired on the owner's word; see Q-AE7. | J (J.3b, J.3c), answered |
| Q27 | Is a one-second age column acceptable for frame comparison? | Yes; it is what every surface prints. | U |
| Q28 | A lock's finer range: fixed, or derived from the plot's age? | Derived, floor 10 m. | D |
| Q29 | Does a baseline-delta check ride the weekly harness job? | Yes, failing at a 20 per cent move; the absolutes stay report-only (D-H-8). | U |
| Q30 | Build the tempban lift, or leave the restart? | Build it on the store route. | L |
| Q31 | dev2: delete or convert? | Delete; somebody with SSH looks at the box first. | L |
| Q32 | Both tracks: content, structure, or both? | Both, in cut M where the hash already moves. | M |
| Q33 | Pay the K6 lease for the three-channel seat now? | No: correct the comment now, build a lease per channel with the first second station. | L |
| Q34 | The matcher: an audit gate now, or a lens now? | The gate and `@find` now; the lens is H6. | U |
| Q35 | Validate the arena's config blocks when `[tacsim].enabled` is off? | Yes, always; see §6. | none |
| Q36 | May `STATUS` print the arena's global tempo word? | No: the pilot's own alert state; the cadence stays in the frame. | D |
| Q37 | What may a player read of a finished match (N2), and is a replay public (N9)? | The report is the player's own projection; the replay of an OOC match is objective and public. | V, I |
| Q38 | Who may `ORDER`, and how wide is the span (N3)? | The side's lead, and any pilot their own squad; 4 squads per commander. Built on this default (Y.1, Y.2), "their own squad" read as the one machine in their pod pair, since a four-pod side is one squad. | Y |
| Q39 | Six a side or twelve (N7)? | Six first. Built on this default (Z.3); twelve is one more pack bump once the fan-out lane has an engaged number. | Z |
| Q40 | Durability's cadence, and do OOC matches resume (N16)? | Every 5,000 ms of arena time (and on the opening pulse); yes. Amended by cut AF's plan: the tail is abandoned behind a `resumed` marker rather than replayed, since a tape records outputs and not inputs, so a resume rewinds the match by up to `snapshot_ms` plus the egress latency (`Q-AF4`). Built on this default (AF.1 to AF.3). | AF |
| Q41 | Does the watchdog void the pulse that finished the match? | Yes: void, and every unit restored (doc 16 §3.1); the forced void is not journalled, the black box keeps the arena's own end. Built on this default (AA.3); reversed by reverting the terminal arm of `Task::pulse` alone. | AA |
| Q42 | Does a reclaimed pilot take the side's lead back (N4)? | No: succession is one-way and the heir keeps it; a side whose every claimant had left gives the lead to the first who comes back. Built on this default (Y.3). | Y |
| Q43 | May a non-lead's order to their wingman replace the lead's live order on that unit (N3)? | Yes, latest-wins per unit, and nobody is told; a per-unit watch or an issuer set is a later cut's if a word should be said. Built on this default (Y.1). | Y |
| Q44 | Should `ORDER <squad> RELEASE` hand a squad back to doctrine and free the span (N3, W8)? | Not in Y: the four arms as N3 lists them, and the span holds until the world's watch expires, since the world never learns an early arena end. Cut Z decides. Decided and built (Z.2): `RELEASE` drops the unit to doctrine at once and the issuer's own watch with it, freeing the span. | Y, Z |
| Q45 — landed M1b.4 | Should the seat get the machine's exact fire cadence through a one-shot armed trigger (a `FIRE` within one cycle of ready recorded as `armed[weapon]`, hashed and snapshotted, acknowledged `cannon armed: fires when ready.` and resolved by the `WeaponReady` due event at `ready_at` inside the substep, as `reflex.rs` already does; one `FIRE` is one shot, cleared by a `LOCK` change or `EXIT`), or should the machines instead pay a seat-like acceptance latency by routing their `FIRE` through phase 4? Today the seat's machine gun fires about every 1,500 ms against the machine's 1,000 and the cannon about every 6,500 against 6,000, a structural rate deficit no client clock closes; `B-86` alone narrows it to a consistent +250 ms. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | The sweep's default was to build the armed trigger (it fits D-16-2 as amended by `Q23` literally, resolving at the pulse that accepted it and scheduling a due event, and it strikes this document's section 6's `fire when ready` row; it moves `state_hash` (a new row), the AF.2 snapshot, the replay and hash fixtures, `help cockpit`'s `FIRE` and Refusals and doc 16 §3.5, §3.7 and §11; size M). Ruled by the owner 2026-10-02: no armed trigger and nothing that fires for the pilot (the owner's weapons direction of the same day: the shipped weapons are placeholders, real weapons have multi-second cycles and precious ammunition, no auto-fire); cadence parity comes the other way, the machines paying the seat's pulse, which the owner's unit-minds decision of 2026-10-02 (AI-filled machines run minds on one code path, thinking on pulse N and acting at N+1; the proposal in `docs/design/proposals/unit-minds.md`, being written) gives by construction. this document's section 6's row stands, with its clause. A harness parity assertion of seat against machine fire rate is still worth adding. | unit minds, open |
| Q46 | May a pilot's own `HEADING` or `SPEED` raise the tempo from `Quiet` to `Alert`? Today the ack `Steering to D°.` prints at forward time, but the arena applies orders in pulse phase 2 and only held contacts in alert range, a queued `FIRE` or an approaching machine raise `Alert`, so before first contact an order can wait up to 5 s to start and shows in the frame of the pulse after, on every client (the TUI and the web too; line mode adds the poll on top, which `N19` answers). (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | Not in a cut; the owner's: one `TempoSignals` flag `pilot_order_queued` stepping `Quiet` to `Alert` (1 s) for `alert_hold_ms`, which changes the pulse partition (`T-05`'s event lists), the `state_hash` sequence and the weekly harness's deterministic block (a baseline row) and raises the `Alert` cost lens, against doc 16 §3.1's tempo table as ruled; `Q23` already accepted pulse-resolved actions. Size S. | open |
| Q47 | Should a match that reaches `max_duration_ms` be decided, and should the default cap stay at an hour? Today any match not ended by elimination, `Spent`, a concession or the hold runs 3,600 s and records `timed out (the clock ran out)` with no winner however one-sided the damage (`outcome_table` has no `(Red, timeout)` row); a machine that loses its target searches the last plot for 60 s and then, after 90 s, crosses to the other side's entry zone, so an immobile pilot off the road (both tracks gone at 75 s) is found only by chance. `B-87` removes most stalemates; this one is independent. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | The sweep's two levers: (a) lower `[tacsim.match].max_duration_ms` to about 900 s (config only, its `mush-config` row; the help states no cap today, `T-92`, so a sentence is added); (b) at `MatchDeadline` settle a winner by a deterministic integer score read once at the deadline's own millisecond (sections destroyed on the other side, then remaining armour and internals per mille, a draw on a tie), giving `red won (timeout on damage)` inside doc 16 §4's six words (it moves `record.rs`'s `outcome_table` tests, the report and replay head lines, `step.rs`'s deadline tests, doc 16 §4 and `help simulator`; not hashed state, but the journalled end changes); optionally `hunt_goal` aims at the last plot older than `search_ms` before the far zone. Size S. Ruled by the owner 2026-10-02: (a) yes, the shipped default drops to 900 s at every default site (the arena is an explicit OOC proving ground); (b) not built now, revisited if timeouts persist in the lanes once `B-87` and the cap land. Built on this ruling (AP.2): (a) at every default site, (b) not. | AP |
| Q48 | Is disengaging meant to be counterplay? The machine stops at about 420 m (aiming at 390), turns nose-on and fires one weapon per 1 s decide tick (`engage_orders` picks a single weapon per tick); a stopped pilot on grass is visible to 480 m, so the only standing room outside the machine's stop and inside concealment is 420 to 480 m, about 60 m; past 600 m the machine enters `Approach` again, and on a lost contact drives straight at the last plot for up to 60 s with no give-up range or pattern, so the bot's re-approach reached its 650 m wait 11 times in 31 and shipped off. A pilot being out-shot can only stand and trade. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep; the evidence is the bot's 31 re-approaches, one pilot style) | The owner rules. If yes: lower `engage_band_permille` to about 600 (a 360 m stop, 120 m of band under the stopped sight; config only; it moves the E.1 range-table test, the 420 m in `help simulator` and doc 16 §6, and the harness baseline), and, with it or instead, end `search_goal` early when the plot is already past `release_cm` plus a margin from the machine's own pose (it reads the plot, never the live pose, per F.3). If no: say so in `help simulator`. Either way `N22`'s machine shots-per-second figure; E.1's hysteresis gap between the closing and the release edges must stay. Size S. | open |
| Q49 | Should pack v6 move the musters off the wall and off the road? Red slot 1 is cell (6, 97), about 65 m from x = 0, every red slot 45 to 65 m from the edge inside a 160 m entry zone, blue mirrored; slot 1 on each side (the solo human's) is authored on the east-west road, so a stopped tank there is seen at 600 m, not 480. The doctrine never needs ground behind it, so the cost falls on a player who wants to open the range: the bot needed a 235 m drive every match to fight at all, and this geometry is a large but unquantified part of its 8-of-10 posed against about 12-of-30 muster gap (ten- and thirty-match figures with overlapping intervals). `VIEW BRIEF`, `STATUS` and the `Held` line already show the edge; the help says nothing about where a side starts (`T-92`). (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | Not without the owner: deeper entry zones (about 32 cells) with slots at x 28 to 30 (blue mirrored) and slot 1 moved onto grass inside the ford rows 93 to 101, in one bump (v6 and a migration chain, applied by hand on dev1 like the owed 3 to 5), with a pack-lint test asserting passable depth behind each slot against the stock tank's sight and stand-off; the alternatives are slots outside the zone or a soft edge (`motion.rs` and the `state_hash` goldens, a doc 16 §3.2 ruling); it moves the reset and custody positions, the e2e and bot fixtures that assume x 6, cut AI's tuned openings and the opening hashes; both sides kept symmetric (D-16-5); the engine step that needs no pack is `B-91`'s astern clause. Size M. | open |
| Q50 | Should own-side units be born identified? Perception has no own-side IFF: a pod-mate in the adjacent entry cell is detected as an unknown `X1` and named only after `classify_after_ms` (3,000) or a mate's mesh share (0.7 to 1.7 s observed), and the start announcement names nobody, so in the first seconds a pilot cannot tell its wingman from an enemy (`Q-AN3`'s wingman half failed on this). (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | The owner's reading of W5 (ratified under D-16-17, `transponders are spoofable`): (a) in the perception pass a target on the observer's own team is born `Classified { team }` at detection, with the same `Classified` ledger line and `Classify` draw (own side only; hostiles still under W5), moving `state_hash`, the perception tests, the harness counters and doc 16 §11's Q.1 entry; or (b) the lobby only, `Match 3 is running. With you: RB.` per side, which does not map `X1` to `RB`. Size S. | open |
| Q51 | May a contact row say whether the contact is moving? A row gives bearing, band, 50 m range and age, never moving, closing or opening, although detection itself used whether the target was moving (`signature_permille`), so a client fits a track from 50 m-rounded plots and a broken machine is followed into its own entry zone. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | The owner's widening of doc 16 §3.6 (every earlier widening was recorded for revisit): `moving: bool` on the plot at sighting (hashed contact state, a harness baseline row), printed `moving` or `still`; the hash-neutral alternative prints `closing` or `opening` on the locked row only, from the lock's fine figure across two frames. Size S. | open |
| Q52 | Should a contact be held longer than three passes, or a lock coast? A contact unrefreshed for `lost_after_passes` × `pass_ms` (3 × 1,000 ms) is held, lock included; past that it is lost, and a return is a fresh X-number that earns identification again (classification p50 3,000 ms), conceding about three machine-gun shots. this document's section 6 already rejects aliasing a former callsign, because it tells the pilot BA is still out there (W5). (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | No code; if wanted, raise `[tacsim.sensors].lost_after_passes` (for example to 5; config, a server-child restart) and measure; a true coast window keeping the lock and the identity on a re-detection near the coasted plot is new hashed contact state and a W5 design change touching perception, the snapshot and `help cockpit` lines 155 to 159. Size S. | open |
| Q53 | Should the engine bound the commander's sense as well as its legality? It enforces six objectives, a TTL of 1 to 600 s, contact ids from the commander's own store, the epoch, `NotReflex` and the human shield, validated twice, but no tactical invariant: a `Withdraw` on a sound hull with a classified hostile in reach is obeyed at once for the whole TTL (up to 10 minutes) with fire held and the rally suppressed, replaceable only by a contacts-changed signal after the 30 s floor. Whether the engine's own commander does what the bot's did (65 per cent withdrawals, mostly on a lost contact) is unmeasured: that figure came from the bot's different prompt and vocabulary, and the engine's prompt already says doctrine is the default. The bot's own vet flew a withdrawal as a hunt, a silent clamp the engine must not copy. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | Measure first (`N22`'s objective mix by squad health band, from the `Ordered` lines in the cost run); if bad, a per-objective TTL ceiling (`Withdraw` and `Hold` capped near the floor, refused by name past it), or a reflex-side `Refusal::OrderUnsound` for a `Withdraw` above `withdraw_permille` holding a classified hostile in reach; the objective is folded into `state_hash`, so what lands moves golden hashes; a human `ORDER` meets the same check unless its issuer is exempted, which is part of the choice; doc 16 §6 and §11. Size S. | open |
| Q54 | How should the help stay true when the config is retuned? Help topics are static markdown read once at boot; the `ReflexConfig` and `MatchConfig` defaults are written twice but guarded by `config_ext`'s parity test, yet an operator's retune of `engage_band_permille`, `rally_ms`, `hunt_after_ms` or `withdraw_permille` leaves `help simulator` wrong with nothing to warn the player (the boarding announce already reads `board_timeout_ms` live). (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | (a) A boot warning naming every help-quoted key set away from its default (boot code only); the alternatives are (b) a `SIM RULES` read verb printing the live doctrine and timing numbers in fixed sentences (a verb, a help line, a `T-45` token) or (c) `{config}` tokens in help topics (a new loader mechanism); pre-launch, with one production box the owner sets, (a) is the cheapest honest step. Size S. | open |
| Q55 | Should the engine offer a fast headless evaluation lane? Arena time is wall time (`Q-U1`): the task anchors on a tokio `Instant` and the tempo changes only how often `step` runs, so the roughly 140 to 160 s of driving before first contact is spent in real time at 5 s pulses; `Arena::step` is pure and the harness drives it at machine speed, but no line-mode client can attach to a harness arena, so a line pilot is measured only at 1:1 (thirty bot matches took hours and the win-rate intervals stayed wide). At k times speed every wall clock (the flood bucket, the bot's 4 lines a second, `HUMAN_ORDER_TTL_MS`, the LLM TTLs, the pre-check's frame age) gives a line pilot k times fewer orders per arena second, so a compressed match measures a different pilot. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | (c) Nothing in the arena clock: shorten the approach with `T-94`'s pose and seed and `Q49`'s muster, and accept 1:1; the alternatives are (a) a staff OOC `@arena/timescale <k>` scaling `elapsed_ms` for matches with no human pilot (honest for machine-against-machine and AI tuning only) or (b) jumping arena time across a `Quiet` stretch with no held contact or pending event to the next due point (cheap, but it breaks the `arena time is wall time` reading and `BOARD`'s clock); (a) or (b) revisits `Q-U1`, the arena task's timing tests, the harness wake-jitter lens and doc 16 §11; OOC only (D-16-3), staff-only. Size M. | open |
| Q56 | How much may a hit notice say about where the round came from? The `Struck` notice already names the facet band the impact's relative bearing selected (W12), so the pilot knows the quadrant; it names no shooter, side or bearing, by the help's explicit ruling (`nothing whatever about who shot you`); in a fight with several machines `turn into the shot` is a guess about who is shooting, and degrees would expose the shooter's position at fire time beyond what the sensors earned. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | (a) Keep the rule and say plainly in the help that the facet is your quadrant (help only; `T-97`'s sentence); the alternatives are (b) an eight-point relative arc on `Struck` from the bearing the band was chosen by (an additive notice field, TUI words gateway-side, no hash if read from the impact record) or (c) naming a held, identified contact when it is the only hostile the platform holds in that arc. Size S. | open |
| Q57 | When a side has no gun left, does the pilot's side lose too, and may a late pilot take over the fill machine? `B-87`'s clause (a side whose every in-action unit was armed at check-out and now has `weapons.is_empty()` is out) applies to the pilot's side by D-16-5 symmetry, which ends the gunless pilot's 50 s limbo at the turret hit but also means a solo pilot loses the instant the turret goes while still able to drive; separately, a pilot dropped at the boarding deadline (`B-99`) might be allowed a grace window to take over the fill machine flying their slot, which needs the dropped slot kept through check-out, the fill unit adopted as the slot's unit, the reclaim's epoch and control path (doc 07 §1.3, doc 16 §3.8) and the board's words under the observer rule. (raised by the 2026-10-02 engine sweep) | The sweep's default: symmetric (the match is decided when one side cannot fire; `EXIT` and `SIM CONCEDE` remain for the pilot who wants out sooner) and no grace takeover (`B-99`'s specific refusal only); both amend doc 16 §3.8 and need a §11 line. Size S. Ruled by the owner 2026-10-02: yes, D-16-5 symmetry, the pilot's side loses the same way (built in AP.2); the late pilot gets `B-99`'s refusal only, no takeover. Built on this ruling (AP.2); `B-99`'s refusal is still owed. | AP |
| Q58 | Should a discrete `FIRE` whose weapon's `WeaponReady` is already past pull the arena task's wake in, quantised to the substep lattice as a `TempoFloor`'s pull-in is, so a pilot's cycle floor becomes the cycle plus the lattice step rather than the cycle plus a wake? (raised by cut AP's plan, not assumed) | Not built: AP.1 ages the mirror and claims no wake (the pre-check forwards at a remainder of nought and the arena takes the `FIRE` at its next wake, the `WeaponReady` wake when the line arrives before it, the wake after when it does not); an `arena_task` change, rate-independent by construction, for a later cut if the owner wants the cadence. | AP, open |
| Q59 | Should the keyframe `WEAPONSTATUS` re-pushes beside its line carry the aged figures? AP.1 ages the line and not the payload, and neither the TUI nor the web counts down, so a panel pilot can read `cannon  ready` on the line beside `cannon  2 s` in the pane until the arena's ready frame. (raised by cut AP.1's judge, not assumed) | Not built: the keyframe keeps the frame's own `ready` and `ready_in_ms` (`FRAME_VERSION` 1); the alternatives are the aged rows threaded into the re-pushed keyframe alone (world-side, no wire change) or the two panels counting down from the frame they hold (gateway and web); pairs with `B-89`'s tenths. | AP, open |
| Q-U1 | Should the arena task pass the SCHEDULED wall to `step`, so arena time is exactly the substep lattice with a late wake carried as lag? (raised by cut U's plan, not assumed) | No for now: arena time stays wall time, the wake targets are lattice points in absolute arena time, the task's deadline is anchored to its base, and the overshoot's cost is measured by `TACSIM_WAKE_JITTER_MS` (3 ms: 81 short substeps in 120 wakes). | U, open |
| Q-U2 | The arena-to-session events-per-pulse bound: the shipped opening burst is 288 in one pulse at 24 pilots (24 keyframes plus eleven contact-notice lines per pilot at the acquisition pass) against the planned 256. (raised by cut AB's reviews, not assumed) | No row floor; width alone. Under 30 rows the cockpit's damage pane goes first as B.3 built it, under 12 rows every screen drops its chrome and keeps the scrollback and the box; `COCKPIT_ROWS` 30 is a documented derivation `draw` never reads. Built at AB.1. | AB, open |
| Q-AB3 | Does the notices pane ride the Right column (weapons, contacts, notices) or the Left as B.3 built it? | The Right, as AB.1 built it: seven inner rows at 120 by 34 and twelve at 160 by 48 instead of two on the Left under the 12-row damage pane, and the cockpit whole from 120 by 30 with the Left column status over damage. The owner may put it back on the Left (two rows at 120 by 34, four at 120 by 36) in a later cut. | AB, open |
| Q-I1 | Does the line printer's one grid budget (78 by 20 printed columns; `VIEW` and `SIM REPLAY` at 39 by 20 cells, two printed columns a cell) fall under the 120-column ruling (`Q-AB1`)? (raised by cut I's plan, not assumed) | No: the ruling names client surfaces built new, and the printer is the line client's, which AB left at 78 by 20; `SIM REPLAY` shares `VIEW`'s window. | Q-O1 | How do the machines behave under Hold, and who governs a squad on dev1? (raised by cut O's plan, not assumed) | Doctrine's own goal: a machine drives to the key spot in place of the hunt and the walk, parks inside the square and fights from there, and an attacking machine stops at `engage_cm` and never drives in to contest a held square, so a human can camp The Crossroads against an AI side that shoots but never enters, the shipped ground's blue machines reaching the spot first; on dev1 the commander's order governs whenever it gives one (the forced tool always answers an order). Built on this default (O.1); reversed by an `Approach` clause that closes into the square. | O, open |
| Q-O2 | Should a range's result occupy the twenty-deep history ring at all, flushing a real match's row, report and replay? | Yes: a range is a match like any other, tagged ` [range]` on the ring, the header and the replay; the flush is the ring's. Built on this default (O.3). | O |
| Q-O3 | Should the report-only, flag-gated Hold lane (`TACSIM_HOLD_LANE=1`, `harness_lance_goal_cost` beside the lance lane's) join the weekly job? | No: run by hand in O.1's gate only. Until answered the flag matches neither `RUN_*` nor `*_RUN`, so `ci_gated_test_inventory` neither registers nor exempts it; a yes renames and inventories it. | O, open |
| Q-AD1 | May a macro's line start with a speech prefix (`@macro w=:waves at $1`)? (raised by cut AD's plan, not assumed) | No: the line starts with a word (`say`, `pose`, `semipose`, `@emit` or a verb), and a prefixed line is refused at definition (`A macro's line starts with a word (say, pose, semipose, @emit), not with " : ; or \.`); a prefix-expanded rewrite would have to skip the later stages the way a typed prefix does, and `AliasOutcome::Rewrite` carries no such flag. Built on this default (AD.1). | AD, open |
| Q-AD2 | Are macros per character or per account? (raised by cut AD's plan, not assumed) | Per character: the object the line is typed as, twenty each; an alt has its own, and nothing is kept per account. Built on this default (AD.1). | AD, open |
| Q-AD3 | May a macro's line be `@wait ...`, the one player verb that re-dispatches a stored line later? (raised by cut AD's plan, not assumed) | No (`A macro cannot queue a command.`): `@macro z=@wait 0=z` would re-arm itself on every fire, hold a wait-queue slot per chain and survive a disconnect, the one way a mortal could make the world task loop. Built on this default (AD.1), and wider than asked: a `$` in a line's first word is refused at definition too (`A macro's first word cannot hold a $; the words after it can.`), because `@macro y=$1 $1` typed `y @wait 0=y` reached the queue past every definition-time check, and the stage re-checks the expansion's verb (`@wait`, `@sshkey`, `@newpassword`, `@newchar`) whatever the document holds. The owner may want `@wait` back with a depth mark. | AD, open |
| Q-AE1 | Widen the harness cohort to the new chassis, or price the CI cohort's ground from the shipped table? (raised by cut AE's plan, not assumed) | No to both: the cohort stays U.1's eight and the CI ground keeps `harness_move_cost` for every row; pricing it from the table would make heavy woods impassable to 262 tanks and could move the CI per-window lens. The lance lane and the sight differential fight on the shipped ground priced from the table since AE.1; neither is baselined. Built on this default (AE.1). | AE, open |
| Q-AE2 | Should a missile be guided, and is 250 m/s its speed? (raised by cut AE's plan, not assumed) | No guidance, 250 m/s: a lock is never held past a sight (600 m own, about 900 m on a shared plot), so a missile is 2.4 to 3.6 s in the air in play and a moving tank walks out from under the one-cell re-validation; the 1.5 km / 6 s case lives in the arena's tests, and the 2 mrad band is reachable only on a shared plot. A slower round is one number in `missile_lr.json`. Built on this default (AE.2). | AE, open |
| Q-AE3 | Does the wheeled row ford shallow water? (raised by cut AE's plan, not assumed) | Yes, at 400 (`locomotion.json`, `LOCOMOTION_DIGEST` re-pinned); rubble, rock and heavy woods still refuse it. Built on this default (AE.1). | AE, open |
| Q-AE4 | Is the class cap per side or per map? (raised by cut AE's plan, not assumed) | Per side: `drone = 1` on each side, doc 16 §2 W2 amended in §11. Built on this default (AE.3). | AE, open |
| Q-AE5 | Where does The Bluff sit? (raised by cut AE's plan, not assumed) | East of the hold square at 141..147 by 105..118, 105 cells from blue's muster and 138 from red's, overlooking The Crossroads: the one flat −200 shelf of its size near the centre. Its rows cross the lance lane's blue rows 105 and 107, and the lane's W8 held at its defaults there, so the decided fallback (rows 109..118, clear of every lance row) was probed and not taken; the owner may move it in a later pack version. Built on this default (AE.1). | AE, open |
| Q-AE6 | With the fill on, may a lone drone pilot start a match? (raised by AE.3's judge, not assumed) | Today no: `ai_side_size` is the larger of the stored size and the largest side, one with one pilot, so `fill_pods` adds nothing beside the claimed drone and `SIM START` reads `The red side fields no gun; a side needs a machine that carries one.` with no hint that `SIM AI 2` starts it. Either `fill_pods` tops a gunless side up by one armed pod (shared by `ready()` and `start()`), or the sentence or `help simulator` gains the `SIM AI 2` hint; the test pins today's behaviour. | AE, open |
| Q-AE7 | Does the 20 ms authored per-window lens move? (raised by AE.3's gate, not assumed) | Not by a builder: the worst asserted run on this host read 17.623 ms (past the 17.241 guard, so the recorded constant stays 17.108) and 17.623 × 1.16 = 20.44, so the lens no longer clears the host's worst run by the 16 per cent spread; the deterministic block was equal in every run and AE.3 runs nothing the cohort runs, so it is host noise, and the doc makes the lens the owner's. Cut AF read above the guard four more times (17.773 and 17.579 in AF.2, 17.940 in AF.3, 18.022 in its sweep; its gates 15.815 and 15.660) with the deterministic block equal every time; nothing moved. Answered by the owner on 2026-09-29 (J.3d, `75309700`): the ratio check is retired; `the_lens_is_the_cohorts_own` holds only that the 20 ms lens is above the worst asserted run on record and under the 25 ms ceiling, and the record follows the worst run that passed (18.022, cut AF's sweep); headroom is read at a review, never asserted. | AE (J.3d), answered |
| Q-AE8 | The authored lane's per-wake p99 at seed 2350 sits about 5 per cent under the 12 ms lens and crossed it once in cut AE's sweep (12.369 ms; 11.15 to 11.63 on five re-runs), and AE.1 already crossed it at seeds 2351 and 2353: which repair? (raised by the sweep, not assumed) | Treat the margin as known for now. The cost is A star cells expanded by `Withdraw` machines re-searching full-width routes to their entry cell: one orbiting its next route cell so Q.7's wandered-off rule clears the route every `decide_ms`, one stopped `impassable` at a corner motion cuts and the shoulder rule skips (the documented class gap), re-searching the same 13,667-cell route every `repath_ms`. The second option is a reflex cut (no re-search while orbiting, a back-off for a machine stopped at the same cell re-planning the same route, the corner-cut gap closed) and a re-bless of the CI baseline; the third bounds path cells per `decide_ms` window beside the query bound. Each moves the deterministic block, so none is a builder's. Cut AF crossed the lens once more (12.078 ms, AF.2's builder run, re-run under rule (a)) and read 10.25 to 11.35 on its passing runs. Closed by cut AI.1 (`0a465976`, 2026-09-29): the second option built (the follower's turning circle, the closed-corner rule, the bounded refusal memory reading nought, the CI baseline re-blessed once) and the third not, the per-wake `path_cells` peak bounded by `[tacsim.harness].max_path_cells_per_pulse` (48,128) as a harness threshold; the authored per-wake p99 read 8.1 to 8.9 ms at seed 2350 on every asserted run of the cut and 10.5 at seed 2351 report-only (from 17.4); 16b section 9, row AI. | AE, closed by AI.1 |
| Q-AF1 | Is the staff tempo floor (`@arena/tempo`) lifted by a resume? (raised by cut AF's plan, not assumed) | Yes: a floor is a lever set for a process that is gone; the clock re-anchors with no floor and `@arena/status` shows none, and staff set it again. Built on this default (AF.1, `Clock::reanchor`; AF.3). | AF, open |
| Q-AF2 | Should a resumed match's pilots be re-seated automatically when their gateway session survived the server-half restart? (raised by cut AF's plan, not assumed) | No: every seat is released at the resume, the machine flies the tank, and the pilot takes it back with `BOARD` through Y.3's reclaim after `reclaim_min_ms`, reading the resume line, the doctrine line and the catch-up. The cost, said plainly: every released tank goes to the reflex controller in `Withdraw` and drives toward its own entry zone until its pilot boards, on both sides at once, while AI-filled tanks keep fighting, so a resume shifts the fight toward the side with more AI fill for `reclaim_min_ms` plus the pilots' return, and the first resumed pulses pay a burst of route searches (the machine fired during the handover in `sim_rematch`). A resume-marked control that releases into a hold or `Patrol` instead of `Withdraw` is a later cut. Built on this default (AF.3). | AF, open |
| Q-AF3 | May the snapshot cadence be shortened under `Engaged`? (raised by cut AF's plan, not assumed) | No: one cadence in arena time (`snapshot_ms`), because the encode cost is the field's size and not the tempo's, and `harness_arena_snapshot` says what it costs (about 12 to 14 ms on the task thread per snapshot of a 300-unit field). Built on this default (AF.2). | AF, open |
| Q-AF4 | A resume rewinds the match to its last snapshot (up to `snapshot_ms` of arena time plus the egress latency): a hit, a lock or a wreck a pilot read in the abandoned seconds un-happens, and the tape keeps those seconds only as an abandoned tail behind a marker; a planned reload rewinds too, because no final snapshot is taken on the way down (`arena_shutdown` runs after the world task has stopped). Accepted? (raised by cut AF's plan, not assumed) | Accepted: a pilot whose seat the resume released reads one line, `The server restarted and the match resumed from its last save; what happened after that save did not count.`, before the doctrine line. A final snapshot at shutdown is a later cut. Built on this default (AF.3). | AF, open |
| Q-AF5 | Does the inline encode's cache cost on the wake after it move a lens? (conditional, raised by cut AF's plan, to be asked only if AF.2's off-and-on measurement showed it) | Not raised: off against on (per wake / per window p99) read CI 5.625 / 10.059 off and 6.226 / 10.562 on, authored 11.254 / 16.550 off and 10.401 / 15.958 on, no lens move attributable to the encode; the figure itself (p50 11.2 to 13.5 ms, wake plus encode up to 21.2 ms, the encode cloning every unit's opaque `TacticalUnit` once through `UnitRepr`) is the owner's to see, a borrowing `UnitRepr` serialiser or an incremental snapshot being the lever if it costs. | AF, closed at its default |
| Q-AG1 | Should the brief's terrain words be the legend's labels (`woods_heavy`) rather than prose (`heavy woods`)? (raised by cut AG's plan, not assumed) | Prose, from one pinned exhaustive table (`class_prose`); the legend keeps its labels, the renderer's key to a glyph, and the pinned table is the one place the two vocabularies meet. Built on this default (AG.1). | AG, open |
| Q-AG2 | Should a covered spot (drawn under a contact or the `@`) be named `In the window` as surveyed ground, although `VIEW`'s legend does not name it? (raised by cut AG's plan, not assumed) | No: the brief names the spots the legend names and nothing else, the owner rule read strictly, so a covered spot is named by neither spot line as by neither of `VIEW`'s; a pilot who wants it has `RANGE <spot>` and `STATUS`'s nearest-spot line. Built on this default (AG.1). | AG, open |
| Q-AG3 | Should `VIEW <other word>` keep drawing the map, as it silently did before AG? (raised by cut AG's plan, not assumed) | No: one syntax-first usage line, `VIEW [BRIEF]; BRIEF says the window in sentences.`, the same bytes for every word and never an echo, so a stray designator says nothing a stray word does not. Built on this default (AG.1). | AG, open |
| Q-AG4 | Should the brief reach a screen (a pane of its own in the TUI, a web pane)? (raised by cut AG's plan, not assumed) | Not in AG: line-mode text rung as `CONTACTS` is; it reaches the TUI notices pane and the web ring as `Sys` text by construction, and a pane of its own is a gateway-half and `web/` cut of its own. Built on this default (AG.1). | AG, open |
| Q-AH1 | Should the once-per-account hint flags live on the account record (`AccountRec`, a new `ObjectStore` method and a boot-hydrated mirror) rather than on a tacsim-namespace document keyed by `AccountId`? (raised by cut AH's plan, not assumed) | The document: one `tacsim:tutorial:<account>` on the module's own `push_op` lane beside its pins, loaded by `store::load` before the resume's phase A and inert under a rollback; the account record is reachable only through the staff executor's async `set_account_*` methods with no RAM mirror a world command could read. Built on this default (AH.1). | AH, open |
| Q-AH2 | Should `@tutorial on` (or a `reset`) re-arm hints already read? (raised by cut AH's plan, not assumed) | No: once means once; `on` clears the switch and the ones read stay read; no `reset`. Built on this default (AH.1). | AH, open |
| Q-AH3 | Should the lobby hint fire for staff walking in? (raised by cut AH's plan, not assumed) | Yes: a person is a person, and a staffer who never enters the lobby reads none (God reads it in `sim_solo`'s `open_the_centre` and holds a document of his own). Built on this default (AH.1). | AH, open |
| Q-AH4 | Should a hint be marked shown when the actor has no live connection? (raised by cut AH's plan, not assumed) | No: `hint` returns before touching the record when `World::has_live_conns` is false (a linkdead pilot is still `is_connected` and keeps receiving frames), so the point waits for its next occasion. Built on this default (AH.1). | AH, open |
| Q-AH5 | Should the switch be a selector of `SIM` (`SIM HINTS on` and `SIM HINTS off`) rather than a verb of its own? (raised by cut AH's plan, not assumed) | `@tutorial`: `SIM` is `authoritative` and refused outside the three centre rooms, while the switch must work from Limbo and a pod, and the programme lead named the verb. Built on this default (AH.1). | AH, open |
| Q-AJ1 | What win rate against the machines counts as the cut working? (raised by cut AJ's entry) | The end-to-end test asserts a finished match with hits scored and an honest report, never a win; the ten-match 1 v 1 lane is report-only with a target of seven wins in ten, its figure recorded in the AJ row. Built on this default (AJ.2 and AJ.4): the lane ran once on 2026-09-29 and read 4 of 10 (lost 1, timed out 5), recorded in the AJ row. | AJ, open |
| Q-AJ2 | May the bot give `ORDER`s to its side's machines when it leads? | No in AJ: it flies its own tank; commanding squads is a later cut. Built on this default (AJ.2): the lobby's closed vocabulary holds no `ORDER`. | AJ, open |
| Q-AJ3 | May the bot lead a side? | Only when it is first on the side; under a human lead it never types `SIM START`, `SIM AI`, `SIM UNIT` or `SIM CONCEDE`. Built on this default (AJ.2 and AJ.4): the lead is the side's first claimant still holding a slot, `SIM START` waits a grace, and the wingman test proves the bot never starts under a human lead. | AJ, open |
| Q-AJ4 | How fast may the bot type? | At most 4 lines a second, well under the session flood bucket (10 a second, 30 burst) and the cockpit's 8 pending intents a tank. Built on this default (AJ.2): a ring limiter on the lane's own clock, `[arena].max_lines_per_s` 4 (1 to 4). | AJ, open |
| Q-AJ5 | What becomes of what the bot learns about beating the machines? | Recorded in the AJ row as input for a later machine cut; AJ changes nothing in `reflex`. Built on this default (AJ.4): the reading is in the AJ row's Landed and Deviations cells; `reflex` is unchanged. | AJ, open |
| Q-AK1 | How far may the bot chase a contact it has lost? (raised by cut AK's entry) | To the last plot, then along the contact's estimated course for as long as the plot is younger than a bound, then one bounded sweep; never past the arena's edge; any fresh plot of a hostile ends the hunt and engages. Built on this default (AK.1): the hunt goes to the last plot, then along the track or the opening bearing at an assumed 8 m/s while the plot is under 90 s old (AK.3 raised it from 45 s), then one box sweep of four 300 m legs within 60 s, every aim 60 m inside the edge; a fresh hostile ends it and a kill drops the trail. | AK, open |
| Q-AK2 | May the commander still order a withdrawal? | Yes, but a `withdraw` doctrine is a bounded hold (the planner sets the bound, at most 30 s) that the pilot drops when it lapses or the commander stops, and its prompt says withdrawal is for a failing hull, never for a lost contact. Built on this default (AK.2): `hold_s` clamped to 5 to 30 s (10 when absent), counted once per episode, then a 30 s rest; kept only on a failing hull, otherwise flown as `hunt` or `orbit` and recorded as `commander-overruled`; cleared by any stop of the commander. | AK, open |
| Q-AK3 | What match cap does the win-rate lane measure on? | 420 s a match (the game's own default is an hour); a timeout is reported as its own column, never as a loss, and the lane's bound grows to match. Built on this default (AK.3): `MAX_DURATION_MS` 420,000 with `max_seconds` 540, one 100-minute deadline, and the `bot-win-rate` job bound at 150 minutes; the three runs took 1,447 to 1,900 s. | AK, open |
| Q-AK4 | May a test spend real model money? | Only an ignored lane that runs when `MUSH_BOT_LIVE_COMMANDER=1` and a real `OPENROUTER_API_KEY` are both set by the person running it; CI never sets them, and no builder runs it. Built on this default (AK.3): `arena_live_commander_five_matches` is ignored, skips unless `MUSH_BOT_LIVE_COMMANDER` is exactly `1`, fails when opted in without a key, and is named by a ci.yml comment and run by no job; no test, builder, reviewer, gate or sweep called a real model. | AK, open |
| Q-AK5 | What figure counts as AK working? | Report-only as in Q-AJ1: the no-model lane's target stays seven wins in ten, now on the 420 s cap; the figure, each tuning round's figure and the lead's live commander figure are recorded in the AK row. Built on this default (AK.3): three no-model runs read 2, 4 and 4 of 10, 4 of 10 selected and recorded in the AK row with every run; the lead's live commander lane, flown 2026-09-30 after the record, read 1 of 5 (3 lost, 1 timed out) for 0.0626 US dollars, carried in cut AL's entry. | AK, open |
| Q-AL1 | Should the bot still circle its target at all? (raised by cut AL's entry) | `orbit` stays a stance the commander may choose, but the pilot's default engagement becomes `hold`: stopped at the band, bow within 30 degrees of the hostile, range changed along the line of fire. Built on this default (AL.2): `stand_off` renamed `hold` (a model's `stand_off` still read) and `[arena] engagement` (`hold` by default, `orbit` kept as a stance and the forced-orbit run's knob); the hold stops at the band with the bow on the hostile, `HOLD_BOW_DEG` 30, and changes range only along the line of fire with a 25 m hysteresis; AL.3 gave the commander `hold` and flies an overruled withdraw as the engagement. | AL, open |
| Q-AL2 | How many refused `FIRE` lines are worth a full rate of fire? | At most one line in ten refused as `Still cycling`; the guns' rate comes first (the machine gun's median gap at most 1,200 ms, the cannon's at most 6,500 ms). Built on this default (AL.1), with one ruling: the no-pulse and 250 ms lattice rigs assert at most one `Still cycling` line in ten per gun and the medians on FIRE lines and accepted shots; on the 500 ms pulse rig the programme lead's ruling of 2026-09-30 (three runs read the cannon refused 3 lines of 21) allows each gun `lines / 10` plus the slack's climb, `(FIRE_SLACK_MAX_MS - FIRE_SLACK_START_MS).div_ceil(FIRE_SLACK_UP_MS)`, 2 at the built constants, looser than one in ten and than AK's 5 per cent over both guns. The tests admit `FIRE_SLACK_START_MS` 50 to 300 (AL.1 recorded 50 to 410; AL.3's lane tests fail at 350), `FIRE_SLACK_UP_MS` 100 to 200, `FIRE_RETRY_MS` 150 to 500 and `WEAPONS_LEARNED_MS` 6,001 to 10,000, each found one constant at a time. Live (AL.3) the one in ten was missed for both guns in every run (the selected run's cannon 12 of 104, machine gun 58 of 423; the machine gun's accepted-shot median about 1,500 ms against a line-mode floor of about 1,250), and closing it needs a slack past the one-pulse cap. | AL, open |
| Q-AL3 | May the bot read the ground to pick an ambush (woods, rubble) in this cut? | No: it stops before it is found on whatever ground it stands on, from what its own lines tell it; choosing ground is a later cut. Built on this default (AL.3): no ground is read; the pilot stops wherever it stands, facing a return not yet told apart (6 s, a rest as long, four a match) and, for a hunted machine it has not hurt and that is not going away, waiting 20 s once its estimate is within 700 m; the AL.3 bullet's per-ground ceilings are not built. | AL, open |
| Q-AL4 | What figure counts as AL working? | Report-only as in Q-AJ1 and Q-AK5: the target stays seven wins in ten on the 420 s cap; every run's figure, the forced-orbit comparison run and the facet split of the hits taken are recorded in the AL row. Built on this default (AL.3): the hold read 5, 5 and 4 of 10 (4 of 10 selected on the guns' refusals) and the forced orbit 4 of 10 (timed out 1); on the hold every facet hit taken struck a front facet, under the orbit 47 of 429; every run is recorded in the AL row; the lead's live commander figure on the new policy is still owed. | AL, open |
| Q-AM1 | How slow a wire must the bot survive? (raised by cut AM's entry) | One-way delays with jitter up to 300 ms, a reply split across writes, and a line dropped into the middle of a reply; the latency rig asserts the picture, the lock and the fire clock hold there, and loopback stays the default rig. Built on this default (AM.1): the rig (`Wire`: a 40 ms base delay, up to 300 ms jitter, split gaps of 160 to 300 ms, three pieces per multi-line chunk, chatter or `Receiver clear.` in one reply in three, TCP order kept both ways) reads a mean round trip of 821 ms on its seed (the down leg's mean 625, max 1,704), against the plan's about 380, because the down leg queues each chunk behind the last one's pieces; there the picture and the lock hold at every tick, no hunt starts and no `FIRE` is refused as still cycling, but neither gun's cycle is learned (0 of 8 and 0 of 18) and the machine gun fires 19 times a minute against about 55 on loopback; a cycle learned through the send bound at a fast round trip is shown only by a picture unit test. | AM, open |
| Q-AM2 | What does `/pause` do to a bot that is flying? | It stops typing and nothing else: the lane keeps reading, and `/resume` restarts every lane deadline from the resume instant so no step lapses while paused; a paused tank sitting in a match is the person's choice. Built on this default (AM.2), sharpened: the lane types nothing while paused and the lobby reads every line at the pause instant, `/resume` moving every lobby wait on by the pause's length (each continues where it left off; the resume sentence still says `every wait starts again from now`) while the match's own times stay real; `/send` still pauses the bot; a pause spanning a match's whole boarding window and its start still blocks on `BOARD` after the resume (an AM residual). Since AO.2 the runner ticks a paused lane (it reads and settles, the commander silent), the resume sentence says the lobby's waits go on where they stood, and a pause through a boarding window and the start loses that match (said, recorded `left`, the next claimed) instead of blocking, the game having dropped the slot. | AM and AO.2, open |
| Q-AM3 | May a stopped bot wait before it leaves the centre? | Yes, for the answer to a claim or start already in flight, at most one lobby step's timeout; then one `SIM LEAVE` whatever it learned, so a stopped bot never holds or leads a slot. Built on this default (AM.2): only a stop waits (a blocker or a match's end closes at once), until the line's typing plus `[arena].step_timeout_ms`, then one `SIM LEAVE` even while the centre boards or runs, none when the answer names the other side's slot (not this run's to give up); `App::arena_finish` waits at most `Lane::stop_bound_ms` (the step timeout, `LEAVE_WAIT_MS` and 500 ms). | AM, open |
| Q-AN1 | May the bot act on what `help simulator` says the machines do? (raised by cut AN's entry) | Yes: it is player help any pilot can read; the bot still never reads `reflex`, and a behaviour the help does not describe is learned only from the bot's own transcripts. Built on this default (AN.2, AN.3): the opening's hold, the halt, the re-approach past the machine's 600 m sight and the follow of a broken machine outside its 200 m and its one return are taken from `help simulator`'s Fighting the machines and `help cockpit`'s sight rules, and the commander's policy states them; nothing of `reflex` was read. | AN, open |
| Q-AN2 | Which win rate is the figure of record from AN on? | The muster lane's (no pose, 600 s cap), since it is the match a person plays; the posed lane stays as the duel benchmark and both are recorded. Built on this default (AN.1): `arena_muster_rate` (ten matches, no pose, a 600 s cap, a 140-minute deadline), run only by CI's manual `bot-muster-rate` job, never dispatched; it read 4 of 10 at the baseline and 7, 5 and 2 of 10 on AN.3's default runs (8 of 10 with the re-approach off), the posed duel 4 of 10. | AN, open |
| Q-AN3 | May the bot open by waiting near its own entry rather than advancing? | Yes, by default, behind an `[arena]` knob; a wingman under a human lead waits the same way (keeping station on a human's tank is a later cut). Built on this default in part (AN.2): `[arena] opening` (`wait` by default, `search` the old drive) holds stopped near the own entry, bow toward the other side's; the wingman half is not met, since a wingman beside a teammate at the muster sees an untold return on its first `CONTACTS` and gets no opening; a lead's ruling is owed. AO.1 keeps the wingman's opening: a contact the stream names as the own side, or an unidentified one within 100 m, no longer ends it; keeping station on a human's tank is still a later cut. | AN and AO.1, open |
| Q-AO1 | How is a knob's default chosen when runs disagree? (raised by cut AO's entry) | A behaviour stays on by default only when its own reading line shows it doing what it is for (the re-approach reaching its wait, the lattice refusing fewer lines) and its pooled figure on is not below its figure off; otherwise it ships off and stays a knob. Built on this default (AO.3): the re-approach reached its wait in 11 of 31 back-offs against the 16 its line required and ships off (`[arena] reapproach = false`, still a key); the lattice refused 6.9 per cent of `FIRE` lines against 15.9 off at an equal 1,500 ms shot gap and stays on; no figure was below its figure off. | AO, open |
| Q-AO2 | How many matches make a figure? | Thirty, three runs of ten pooled, for the default; ten for an ablation; each printed with a 95 per cent Wilson interval, and no difference called inside overlapping intervals. Built on this default (AO.3): three default runs read 2, 4 and 6 of 10, pooled 12 of 30 (25 to 58 per cent); the re-approach off and the lattice off 3 of 10 each (11 to 60); nothing called; every figure of the two win-rate lanes prints its `WIN RATE INTERVAL`, and the pool lane refuses runs flown with different knobs. | AO, open |
| Q-AO3 | Where may the opening hold? | Where at least the longest back-off the bot can order (to 650 m of a plot from about 420 m) fits behind it on passable ground (on the side away from the other entry, along the line between the two entries), read from `VIEW BRIEF`; when the muster has no such ground the bot steps inward toward the other entry until it does. Built on this default (AO.1), with a back-off knob on: 290 m wanted (a 30 m shortfall taken as enough), up to three legs in along the line between the entries, each ended by a stop and a further `VIEW BRIEF`; from the shipped muster one leg, the first contact then a median 137 s after power against AN's 158 s. | AO, open |

---

## 6. Not doing

| Item | Reason |
|---|---|
| Skip the arena config validators when `[tacsim].enabled` is off (`mush-server/src/config_ext.rs:375-378`) | Doc 16 §11's Q.7 entry states the behaviour as intended; a stale value refused by name today is cheaper than one that surprises the operator the day the layer is switched on. |
| Move N.2 deliberation onto the K7 durable decision pipeline | A recorded deviation with a three-point case at `tacsim:deliberation/mod.rs:54-88`; since 16b cut AF a match survives a server-half restart, but a deliberation in flight dies with the process and the squad is re-asked at its next trigger about the field as it then stands, so durability would preserve the wrong thing (the second argument rewritten by AF.3). Doc 16 §6 is amended in `T-75` and again by cut AF's record. |
| A true match pause | A fourth clock state and a hole in arena time that `lagged` models differently; `@arena/tempo` and the replay answer what a pause is asked for. |
| Sliding along an obstacle after a terrain refusal | Doc 16 §3.4 step 4 is literal and test-pinned, and the pose clamp is what keeps "no unit occupies an untested cell"; the occupancy half of `B-46` is the part that hurts in play. |
| The absolute substep lattice | L, moves every timestamp, and buys nothing at any shipped speed; amended instead (`T-05`, Q24). |
| Field repair and damage control | The verb existed (`+dc`) and D-16-20 deleted it; W15 keeps fights short because units withdraw, and a tank that repairs itself does not have to. Its one good idea, shedding heat by stopping, already happens. |
| Hull-down posture | It moves the hit curve of every shot, every resolution fixture and every hash for one decision; not before `EMCON` has shown whether pilot agency of that kind is wanted. |
| Smoke | It needs a persistent volume in the line-of-sight trace per cell per time, and neither W4 nor the arena has a place to put one. |
| Infantry | At 10 m cells with one unit per cell the model says nothing about a person. |
| A tutor NPC | A second source of truth to keep in step with every verb; N18's hints quote the registered table. |
| Accepting a former callsign after a rename (`LOCK BA` while the row reads `X2`) | It would tell the pilot BA is still out there, which the byte-identical refusal exists to hide; `B-20` is a notice. |
| A `fire when ready` hold for the machine gun's rhythm | Real, from the smoke, and small; not ranked because a standing fire order is an automation the owner has not asked for. It is one paragraph inside N3 if wanted. (Q45: not built; the owner's 2026-10-02 direction is no auto-fire; parity through the unit minds) |
| The "Locked percentage read at the pulse boundary" seed | The smoke's web frames show `lock_percent` tracking range pulse by pulse; not a defect. |
| `k9` (a rear-quarter duel ends in `unit_disabled`) and "frame notices duplicate the Sys lines" | Both re-checked at `820ad9af` and found harmless: the match still ends, and no screen carries both a notices ring and a log pane. |
| Re-posing dev2 onto the template | Deleted instead (`T-72`). |
